* **Evenden, G. I.** (1990). *Cartographic projection procedures for the UNIX environment; a user's manual.* *Open-File Report*. DOI: [10.3133/ofr90284](https://doi.org/10.3133/ofr90284).
  * **Scope:** (1990); (1983) | Original `proj` (1983) & `PROJ.3`/`PROJ.4` Library Architecture.
  * **Critical Annotation:** Written at the USGS Woods Hole Science Center to support coastal and marine geophysical mapping, Evenden's 1983 and 1990 reports introduced the open-source C utility and library `proj` (building upon the 1980 FORTRAN General Cartographic Transformation Package, GCTP, by John P. Snyder and Atef A. Elassal). Evenden abstracted forward $(\phi, \lambda) \to (x, y)$ and inverse $(x, y) \to (\phi, \lambda)$ ellipsoidal and spherical projections into a unified command-line filter and C API parameterized by `+proj=...`, `+a=...`, and `+es=...` key-value arguments.
  * **Practitioner Insight:** Explains why early `PROJ` parameters (`+proj=utm +zone=...`) were designed strictly as 2D mathematical map projections on a given ellipsoid rather than full 3D/4D geodetic datum definitions.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 2.

* **Evers, K., & Knudsen, T.** (2017). Transformation pipelines for PROJ.4. *Proceedings of the FIG Working Week 2017: Surveying the World of Tomorrow—From Digitalisation to Augmented Reality*, Helsinki, Finland, 1–16.
  * **Scope:** FIG Working Week 2017 Proceedings (ISBN `978-87-92853-61-5`) | Modern `PROJ` (v5–v9+) 4D Geodetic Transformation Pipeline Architecture (`PROJ` RFC 1/2 & GDAL RFC 73).
  * **Critical Annotation:** Evers and Knudsen (joined by Even Rouault and Howard Butler in `PROJ` 6+ and GDAL 3+) architected the modern late-binding overhaul of `PROJ`. They replaced Frank Warmerdam's legacy `PROJ.4` early-binding `+towgs84` hub architecture with an arbitrary, daisy-chained four-dimensional transformation pipeline (`+proj=pipeline +step ...`) capable of chaining time-dependent 14-parameter Helmert transformations, horizontal deformation grids (`hgridshift`), vertical geoid/tidal separation grids (`vgridshift`, `tinshift`), and cartographic projections directly between any source and target CRS via an embedded SQLite EPSG/ISO 19111 registry (`proj.db`) and Cloud-Optimized GeoTIFF Geodetic Transformation Grids (GTG).
  * **Key Formulations & Error Budgets:** Eliminates the $1.0\text{--}2.2\text{ m}$ early-binding hub error introduced when legacy software forced transformations between two high-accuracy regional frames (e.g., NAD83(2011) and NAD83(CSRS), or NAD27 and NAD83(2011)) to pivot through a generic WGS84 hub (`+towgs84=0,0,0`).
  * **Practitioner Insight:** Explains the internal mechanics of `projinfo`, `cct`, `cs2cs`, and `gdalwarp -ct`, empowering practitioners to inspect, audit, and pin exact multi-step geodetic pipelines rather than relying on opaque black-box defaults.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 2, Chapter 4.

### F

* **Facciolo, G., Franchis, C. D., & Meinhardt, E.** (2015). *MGM: A Significantly More Global Matching for Stereovision.* *Procedings of the British Machine Vision Conference 2015*, 90.1-90.12. DOI: [10.5244/C.29.90](https://doi.org/10.5244/C.29.90).
  * **Mathematical & Geometric Synthesis:** Formulates **More Global Matching (`MGM`)**, replacing `SGM`'s independent 1D scanline recursions—which suffer from radial "streaking" artifacts across low-texture zones—with a 2D quadrant dynamic programming recursion that couples adjacent scanline costs in both horizontal and vertical directions simultaneously.
  * **DEM Generation & Error Significance:** Dramatically suppresses streaking blunders and improves sub-pixel disparity fidelity on low-contrast snowfields, glaciers, desert sand dunes, calm water boundaries, and planetary regolith.
  * **Operational & Software Implementation:** Serves as the default high-precision matcher (`--stereo-algorithm asp_mgm`) in `NASA ASP` and the `S2P` / `CARS` satellite stereo pipelines.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 8.

* **Farr, T. G., Rosen, P. A., Caro, E., Crippen, R., Duren, R., Hensley, S., Kobrick, M., Paller, M., et al.** (2007). *The Shuttle Radar Topography Mission.* *Reviews of Geophysics*, 45(2), 2005RG000183. DOI: [10.1029/2005RG000183](https://doi.org/10.1029/2005RG000183).
  * **Platform & Kinematic Focus:** Presents the complete mission architecture of the February 2000 Shuttle Radar Topography Mission (SRTM), which deployed a $60\text{ m}$ outboard mast from the Space Shuttle *Endeavour* to acquire single-pass C-band and X-band synthetic aperture radar interferometry (InSAR) between $60^\circ\text{ N}$ and $56^\circ\text{ S}$.
  * **DEM Uncertainty & Error Budget Implications:** Demonstrates how thruster-firing and thermal mast oscillations ($0.2\text{ Hz}$ structural modes) couple directly into interferometric baseline roll angle ($\delta\alpha$), propagating into long-wavelength continental elevation tilts ($\delta z = R \sin\theta\,\delta\alpha$, where $1\text{ arcsec}$ of roll error induces $\sim 1\text{ m}$ of vertical error across the $225\text{ km}$ swath) that had to be constrained via Attitude and Orbit Determination Avionics (AODA) optical metrology and ocean-surface tie passes.
  * **Validation & Operational Guidance:** Establishes the global $90\%$ linear vertical error ($\text{LE90} < 16\text{ m}$ required, $\sim 6\text{–}9\text{ m}$ achieved) and kinematic GPS continent-scale validation benchmarks for near-global spaceborne DEMs.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 7, Chapter 11, Chapter 24.

* **Farrell, W. E.** (1972). *Deformation of the Earth by surface loads.* *Reviews of Geophysics*, 10(3), 761-797. DOI: [10.1029/RG010i003p00761](https://doi.org/10.1029/RG010i003p00761).
  * Referenced in Section 04.02 (Chapter 4) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 4.

* **Federal Geographic Data Committee [FGDC NSSDA]** (1998). *Geospatial Positioning Accuracy Standards, Part 3: National Standard for Spatial Data Accuracy (NSSDA)* (FGDC-STD-007.3-1998). Reston, VA: Federal Geographic Data Committee.
  * *Established standard parametric root mean square error (RMSE) testing and the 95% linear error (LE95) confidence formulation for digital geospatial products.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 12, Chapter 14.

* **Finkel, R. A., & Bentley, J. L.** (1974). *Quad trees a data structure for retrieval on composite keys.* *Acta Informatica*, 4(1), 1-9. DOI: [10.1007/BF00288933](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF00288933).
  * *The foundational computer science paper formalizing the recursive 2D quadtree decomposition underlying multi-scale raster pyramids and octree spatial indexing.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 15, Chapter 16.

* **Fiocco, G., & Smullin, L. D.** (1963). *Detection of Scattering Layers in the Upper Atmosphere (60–140 km) by Optical Radar.* *Nature*, 199(4900), 1275-1276. DOI: [10.1038/1991275a0](https://doi.org/10.1038/1991275a0).
  * Referenced in Section 09.04 (Chapter 9) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 9.

* **Fischler, M. A., & Bolles, R. C.** (1981). *Random sample consensus: a paradigm for model fitting with applications to image analysis and automated cartography.* *Communications of the ACM*, 24(6), 381-395. DOI: [10.1145/358669.358692](https://doi.org/10.1145/358669.358692).
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** A classic `gis-history` automated cartography and computer vision landmark introducing **Random Sample Consensus (`RANSAC`)** to estimate mathematical models (originally the Perspective-3-Point `P3P` camera location problem) from datasets heavily contaminated by gross non-Gaussian outliers (gross false correspondences, swaying branches, moving vehicles/pedestrians, or water-column acoustic multipath). Given an outlier fraction $\varepsilon$ and a minimal solver requiring $s$ points ($s = 3$ non-collinear correspondences for a 6-DoF rigid 3D transform), the number of random trials $N_{\text{iter}}$ required to draw at least one all-inlier minimal sample with confidence $p$ (typically $p = 0.99$) is:
    $$1 - \bigl(1 - (1 - \varepsilon)^s\bigr)^{N_{\text{iter}}} = p \quad \Longrightarrow \quad N_{\text{iter}} = \frac{\ln(1 - p)}{\ln\!\bigl(1 - (1 - \varepsilon)^s\bigr)}$$
  * **Validation and Error Budget:** Whereas ordinary least squares has a breakdown point of $0\%$ (a single gross outlier can corrupt an entire 6-DoF transformation), `RANSAC` and modern polynomial-time graph-theoretic/graduated-non-convexity extensions (`TEASER++` maximum clique and truncated least squares; Yang et al., 2021) reliably recover certifiable coarse 6-DoF alignments even when outlier rates exceed $\varepsilon = 50\%\text{–}95\%$.
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** Essential for coarse global registration of unaligned `TLS` point clouds (via `FPFH` + `RANSAC` or `TEASER++`), ground-plane extraction, and rejecting false loop-closure candidates before inserting factors into a SLAM pose graph.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 6, Chapter 8, Chapter 22.

* **Fisher, P. F.** (1991). *First experiments in viewshed uncertainty: the accuracy of the viewshed area.* Photogrammetric Engineering & Remote Sensing, 57(10), 1321–1327.
  * Seminal paper introducing Monte Carlo viewshed uncertainty analysis and demonstrating the extreme sensitivity of binary visibility to spatially correlated DEM errors.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 33.

* **Fisher, P. F.** (1993). *Algorithm and implementation uncertainty in viewshed analysis.* *International journal of geographical information systems*, 7(4), 331-347. DOI: [10.1080/02693799308901965](https://doi.org/10.1080/02693799308901965).
  * Formalizes the probabilistic "Fuzzy Viewshed" and separates algorithmic ray-interpolation uncertainty from stochastic terrain elevation error.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 33.

* **Fisher, P. F., & Tate, N. J.** (2006). *Causes and consequences of error in digital elevation models.* *Progress in Physical Geography: Earth and Environment*, 30(4), 467-489. DOI: [10.1191/0309133306pp492ra](https://doi.org/10.1191/0309133306pp492ra).
  * *Comprehensive review of DEM error taxonomy, spatial autocorrelation in terrain derivatives, and the propagation of elevation uncertainties into hydrological and geomorphic models.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 14.

* **Florinsky, I. V.** (2016). *Digital Terrain Modeling.* *Digital Terrain Analysis in Soil Science and Geology*, 1-4. DOI: [10.1016/B978-0-12-804632-6.00001-8](https://doi.org/10.1016/B978-0-12-804632-6.00001-8).
  * **Scope:** ISBN: `978-0-12-804632-6` | Differential Geometry of Land Surfaces.
  * **Critical Annotation:** Florinsky delivers one of the most mathematically rigorous treatments of differential geometry applied to twice-differentiable digital elevation surfaces $z = f(x, y)$. The text derives full hierarchies of local, nonlocal, and combined morphometric variables—including mean ($H$), Gaussian ($K$), unsphericity ($M$), difference ($E$), horizontal ($k_h$), and vertical ($k_v$) curvatures—alongside high-order polynomial approximation stencils ($3 \times 3$ and $5 \times 5$ moving windows) and spectral filtering strategies.
  * **Practitioner Insight:** Indispensable for researchers and engineers who must quantify how high-frequency vertical sensor noise $\sigma_z$ amplifies proportionally to $(\Delta x)^{-1}$ in first-order slope derivatives ($\sigma_{\nabla z} \propto \sigma_z / \Delta x$) and $(\Delta x)^{-2}$ in second-order curvature fields ($\sigma_{\nabla^2 z} \propto \sigma_z / \Delta x^2$), as well as how tectonic lineaments and pedological boundaries manifest in multi-scale curvature maps.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 1, Chapter 2, Chapter 18.

  * Derives the Angular Range Analysis (ARA) inversion engine implemented in QPS FMGT (Geocoder), demonstrating objective sediment classification using true DEM grazing angles.

DOI: [10.1016/j.apacoust.2008.09.008](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.apacoust.2008.09.008).
  * Validates Angular Range Analysis (ARA) acoustic themes against ground-truth benthic imagery and geological samples over complex glaciated shelf terrain.

* **Forfinski-Sarkozi, N. A., & Parrish, C. E.** (2019). *Active-Passive Spaceborne Data Fusion for Mapping Nearshore Bathymetry.* *Photogrammetric Engineering & Remote Sensing*, 85(4), 281-295. DOI: [10.14358/PERS.85.4.281](https://doi.org/10.14358/PERS.85.4.281).

  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 11.

* **Forster, C., Carlone, L., Dellaert, F., & Scaramuzza, D.** (2017). *On-Manifold Preintegration for Real-Time Visual--Inertial Odometry.* *IEEE Transactions on Robotics*, 33(1), 1-21. DOI: [10.1109/TRO.2016.2597321](https://doi.org/10.1109/TRO.2016.2597321).
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** Extends Lupton & Sukkarieh's (2012) inertial preintegration to the **rotation Lie group $\text{SO}(3)$**, eliminating Euler-angle singularities and enabling hundreds-of-hertz raw gyroscope ($\tilde{\boldsymbol{\omega}}_k$) and accelerometer ($\tilde{\mathbf{a}}_k$) measurements between consecutive keyframes $t_i$ and $t_j$ to be summarized into a **single compound preintegrated IMU factor** $(\Delta\tilde{\mathbf{R}}_{ij}, \Delta\tilde{\mathbf{v}}_{ij}, \Delta\tilde{\mathbf{p}}_{ij})$ that is completely independent of the global gravity vector $\mathbf{g}^w$ and initial pose $(\mathbf{R}_i, \mathbf{p}_i)$:
    $$\Delta\tilde{\mathbf{R}}_{ij}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}_i^g) = \prod_{k=i}^{j-1} \operatorname{Exp}\!\Bigl(\bigl(\tilde{\boldsymbol{\omega}}_k - \bar{\mathbf{b}}_i^g\bigr)\Delta t\Bigr), \qquad \Delta\tilde{\mathbf{R}}_{ij}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}_i^g + \delta\mathbf{b}_i^g) \approx \Delta\tilde{\mathbf{R}}_{ij}(\bar{\mathbf{b}}_i^g)\,\operatorname{Exp}\!\left(\frac{\partial\Delta\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{ij}}{\partial\mathbf{b}^g}\,\delta\mathbf{b}_i^g\right)$$
    where $\operatorname{Exp}(\boldsymbol{\phi}) = \exp(\boldsymbol{\phi}^\wedge) \in \text{SO}(3)$ is the exponential map, $\bar{\mathbf{b}}_i^g, \bar{\mathbf{b}}_i^a$ are the linearization-point IMU biases, and first-order Jacobians $\partial(\Delta\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{ij}, \Delta\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{ij}, \Delta\bar{\mathbf{p}}_{ij})/\partial(\mathbf{b}^g, \mathbf{b}^a)$ allow the factor graph to update IMU biases during global loop closures **without ever re-integrating the raw $200\text{–}1000\text{ Hz}$ IMU stream**.
  * **Validation and Error Budget:** Derives the exact iterative first-order propagation of discrete-time gyroscope and accelerometer noise ($\boldsymbol{\eta}_k^{gd}, \boldsymbol{\eta}_k^{ad}$) into the preintegrated covariance matrix $\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{ij}^{\text{IMU}} \in \mathbb{R}^{9\times 9}$ on the tangent space of $\text{SO}(3) \times \mathbb{R}^6$, proving sub-millimeter and sub-milliradian agreement with full batch IMU optimization at less than $1\%$ of the computational cost.
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** Forms the core `ImuFactor` / `CombinedImuFactor` in `GTSAM`, `LIO-SAM`, and `Kimera`, binding consecutive LiDAR or camera keyframes together across severe canopy/subterranean motion and keeping roll/pitch locked to the physical gravity vector $\mathbf{g}^w$.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 5, Chapter 6.

* **Francois, R. E., & Garrison, G. R.** (1982). *Sound absorption based on ocean measurements: Part I: Pure water and magnesium sulfate contributions.* *The Journal of the Acoustical Society of America*, 72(3), 896-907. DOI: [10.1121/1.388170](https://doi.org/10.1121/1.388170).
  * *Core Physical Contribution:* Resolves the chemical relaxation mechanisms governing acoustic attenuation in natural seawater, proving that boric acid relaxation dominates between $0.1\text{–}3\text{ kHz}$ and magnesium sulfate dominates between $10\text{–}100\text{ kHz}$.
  * *Engineering & DEM Relevance:* Provides the closed-form analytical equations used by sonar systems and hydrographic software (`MB-System`, `CARIS`, `Qimera`) to compute transmission loss (TL) and calibrate acoustic backscatter intensity for seafloor sediment classification.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 10.

G., et al.** (2013). *Bedmap2: improved ice bed, surface and thickness datasets for Antarctica.* *The Cryosphere*, 7(1), 375-393. DOI: [10.5194/tc-7-375-2013](https://doi.org/10.5194/tc-7-375-2013).

  * Combines satellite radar altimeter hydrostatic inversions with airborne radio-echo sounding to map basal marine ice accretion where radio-echo sounding is absorbed by saline ice.

T., Vaze, P., Sengenes, P., Vinogradova-Shiffer, N., et al.** (2024). *The Surface Water and Ocean Topography Mission: A Breakthrough in Radar Remote Sensing of the Ocean and Land Surface Water.* *Geophysical Research Letters*, 51(4), e2023GL107652. DOI: [10.1029/2023GL107652](https://doi.org/10.1029/2023GL107652).
  * **Platform & Kinematic Focus:** Describes the SWOT spacecraft's Ka-band Radar Interferometer (`KaRIn`), which uses two antennas separated by a rigid $10\text{ m}$ boom to measure 2D water-surface elevation across two $50\text{ km}$ swaths at near-nadir incidence angles ($0.6^\circ\text{–}3.9^\circ$).
  * **DEM Uncertainty & Error Budget Implications:** Demonstrates how near-nadir wide-swath radar interferometry achieves centimeter-level water-surface elevation precision across rivers, lakes, estuaries, and coastal oceans while requiring ultra-stable spacecraft roll knowledge ($< 0.01\text{ arcsec}$ over short wavelengths) and cross-over phase calibration.
  * **Validation & Operational Guidance:** Establishes global calibration and validation protocols combining ocean cross-overs, GNSS buoys, and river stage networks for wide-swath water-surface DEMs.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 7.

* **Fujiwara, T., Kodaira, S., No, T., Kaiho, Y., Takahashi, N., & Kaneda, Y.** (2011). *The 2011 Tohoku-Oki Earthquake: Displacement Reaching the Trench Axis.* *Science*, 334(6060), 1240-1240. DOI: [10.1126/science.1211554](https://doi.org/10.1126/science.1211554).
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** Differences pre-earthquake (1999 and 2004) and post-earthquake (March 2011) **multibeam echosounder (MBES) bathymetric DEMs** across the Japan Trench axis ($\sim 7{,}500\text{ m}$ water depth). By cross-correlating abyssal bathymetric relief features across the lower landward trench slope (which dips seaward toward the east-southeast, $+x$, with positive-up elevation gradient $\partial z_0/\partial x = -\partial d_0/\partial x \approx -0.08\text{ m/m}$), the authors resolve $\Delta u_x \approx +50\text{ m}$ of horizontal east-southeast translation of the outer-rise accretionary wedge right to the trench axis, accompanied by $+7\text{ to }+10\text{ m}$ of net Eulerian vertical seabed uplift ($\Delta z_{\text{Eulerian}} > 0$, or net bathymetric shoaling $\Delta d_{\text{Eulerian}} = -\Delta z_{\text{Eulerian}} \approx -7\text{ to }-10\text{ m}$):
    $$\Delta z_{\text{Eulerian}}(x,y) = -\Delta d_{\text{Eulerian}}(x,y) = \Delta u_z(x,y) - \nabla_H z_0(x,y)\cdot\Delta\mathbf{u}_H(x,y) = (+3\text{ to }+5\text{ m}) - (-0.08\text{ m/m})(+50\text{ m}) \approx +7\text{ to }+9\text{ m}$$
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** Demonstrates the physical mechanism by which horizontal tectonic advection ($\Delta\mathbf{u}_H$) of a sloping continental margin ($|\nabla_H z_0| \approx 0.08\text{–}0.10\text{ m/m}$) nearly doubles the net Eulerian vertical seabed elevation change and drives impulsive tsunami wave generation.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 4.

* **Fukushima, T.** (2006). *Transformation from Cartesian to Geodetic Coordinates Accelerated by Halley’s Method.* *Journal of Geodesy*, 79(12), 689-693. DOI: [10.1007/s00190-006-0023-2](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00190-006-0023-2).

### G

* **Galloway, D. L., & Burbey, T. J.** (2011). *Review: Regional land subsidence accompanying groundwater extraction.* *Hydrogeology Journal*, 19(8), 1459-1486. DOI: [10.1007/s10040-011-0775-5](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10040-011-0775-5).
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** Comprehensive hydrogeodetic synthesis of aquifer-system compaction driven by groundwater and hydrocarbon extraction across major alluvial and lacustrine basins worldwide (including California's San Joaquin and Santa Clara valleys, Mexico City, Jakarta, Houston–Galveston, Venice, and the North China Plain). Formulates Terzaghi's effective-stress principle ($\sigma'_e = \sigma_T - p$, where $\sigma_T$ is total overburden stress and $p$ is pore-fluid pressure) and skeletal specific storage ($S_{sk} = S_{ske} + S_{skv}$, in $\text{m}^{-1}$), distinguishing recoverable seasonal elastic deformation ($S_{ske}$, mm-to-cm scale) from permanent, irreversible inelastic clay-aquitard compaction ($S_{skv} \approx 30\text{–}100\,S_{ske}$) when hydraulic head drops by $\Delta h_{\text{head}} = h_{\text{head}}(t) - h_{\text{head}}(t_0) < 0$ (in $\text{m}$) below the historical preconsolidation head threshold across a compressible aquitard of initial thickness $b_0$ (in $\text{m}$), producing positive-upward surface elevation change $\Delta z_{\text{surf}} < 0$ (equal to negative downward compaction $-\Delta b$):
    $$\Delta z_{\text{surf}} = -\Delta b = b_0 S_{skv}\,\Delta h_{\text{head}} \qquad (\text{e.g., } b_0 = 100\text{ m},\ S_{skv} = 1.5\times 10^{-3}\text{ m}^{-1},\ \Delta h_{\text{head}} = -30\text{ m} \implies \Delta z_{\text{surf}} = -4.5\text{ m})$$
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** Explains why anthropogenic subsidence rates ($50\text{–}300\text{ mm yr}^{-1}$) frequently exceed tectonic and GIA rates by two orders of magnitude, why static national velocity grids (`HTDP`) cannot capture localized pumping bowls, and why multi-temporal InSAR (`MintPy`) time series must be fused with LiDAR DEMs to maintain valid flood-control and canal-grade elevations.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 4.

  * *Core Engineering Contribution:* Documents the architecture of the second-generation $90^\circ$-swath Krupp Atlas *Hydrosweep DS* multibeam echosounder ($15.5\text{ kHz}$, 59 beams) and the *Parasound* parametric sub-bottom profiler, introducing active cross-fan transmission to invert mean water-column sound velocity in real time.
  * *Engineering & DEM Relevance:* Overcame the $\tan^2\theta$ outer-beam refraction barrier that previously restricted deep-ocean multibeams to $45^\circ$ swaths, doubling deep-sea mapping coverage while maintaining outer-beam vertical accuracy.

  * Exhaustive formulation of hydrostatic buoyancy equilibrium for floating ice shelves, deriving the sensitivity of ice thickness to firn air content ($h_{\text{air}}$) and seawater density.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 30.

* **Grodecki, J., & Dial, G.** (2003). *Block Adjustment of High-Resolution Satellite Images Described by Rational Polynomials.* *Photogrammetric Engineering & Remote Sensing*, 69(1), 59-68. DOI: [10.14358/PERS.69.1.59](https://doi.org/10.14358/PERS.69.1.59).
  * **Mathematical & Geometric Synthesis:** Establishes the standard **bias-compensated Rational Polynomial Coefficient (RPC) block adjustment** method for agile linear-array pushbroom satellites (`IKONOS`, `WorldView`, `Pléiades`, `Planet SkySat`), where the physical sensor model is encapsulated in ratios of 20-term third-order polynomials mapping normalized $(\varphi, \lambda, h)$ to image line/sample $(l, s)$.
  * **DEM Generation & Error Significance:** Proves that star-tracker attitude and ephemeris errors manifest primarily as an image-space translation or 6-parameter affine drift $(\Delta l = a_0 + a_s s + a_l l,\; \Delta s = b_0 + b_s s + b_l l)$; solving these parameters via bundle adjustment with tie points and minimal GCPs (or ICESat-2 laser altimetry) reduces absolute satellite DEM geolocation errors from $3\text{–}15\text{ m}$ to the sub-pixel ($<0.5\text{ m}$) level.
  * **Operational & Software Implementation:** Implemented directly in `GDAL` (`RPC00B` metadata tags), `NASA ASP` (`bundle_adjust`), `Orfeo ToolBox (OTB)`, and `BAE SOCET GXP`.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 7, Chapter 8.

* **Gröger, G., & Plümer, L.** (2012). *CityGML – Interoperable semantic 3D city models.* *ISPRS Journal of Photogrammetry and Remote Sensing*, 71, 12-33. DOI: [10.1016/j.isprsjprs.2012.04.004](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.isprsjprs.2012.04.004).
  * *Foundational architectural paper establishing semantic-geometric coherence, Terrain Intersection Curves (`TIC`), and multi-scale urban ontologies in CityGML.*

  * *The international open standard defining multi-scale semantic 3D vector modeling of urban topography, buildings, bridges, and tunnels across Levels of Detail (LoD 0–4).*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 17.

* **Grohman, G., Kroenung, G., & Strebeck, J.** (2006). Filling SRTM voids: The delta surface fill method. *Photogrammetric Engineering & Remote Sensing*, 72(3), 213–216.
  * *Introduces the Delta Surface Fill (`DSF`) paradigm for seamlessly warping auxiliary elevation data across large radar layover and shadow voids without perimeter cliffs.*

* **Guenther, G. C.** (1985). *Airborne Laser Hydrography: System Design and Performance Factors* (NOAA Professional Paper Series, National Ocean Service 1). Rockville, MD: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration. (405 pp.). https://repository.library.noaa.gov/view/noaa/2812
  * *Core Physical & Mathematical Contribution:* Universally known among hydrographers as "the Guenther Bible," this monumental 405-page monograph establishes the complete physical and mathematical foundation for green-wavelength ($532\text{ nm}$) airborne laser bathymetry (ALB). Guenther derives the 3D vector Snell's law refraction geometry across a dynamic, wind-roughened air–water interface, proves that maintaining a constant off-nadir circular scan angle near $\theta_a \approx 15^\circ\text{–}20^\circ$ minimizes random wave-slope depth biases while avoiding specular nadir glint saturation, and models Monte Carlo multiple forward scattering in turbid coastal waters.
  * *Engineering & DEM Relevance:* Quantifies the "propagation-induced depth bias" (geometric path elongation and temporal skewness of the diffuse photon cone in the water column) as well as receiver hardware time-walk, providing the analytical look-up corrections that remain embedded in operational hydrographic processing suites to meet IHO *S-44* Total Vertical Uncertainty (TVU) standards.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 9.

  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 9.

  * *Core Geodetic & Stochastic Contribution:* Establishes the rigorous geodetic error-propagation and spatial covariance framework for repeat-pass and bistatic InSAR, quantifying turbulent and stratified tropospheric water-vapor phase screens, orbital baseline uncertainty, and geometric/temporal decorrelation.

* **Hare, R., Godin, A., & Mayer, L. A.** (1995). *Accuracy Estimation of Canadian Swath (Multibeam) and Sweep (Multi-Transducer) Sounding Systems*. Fredericton, NB / Ottawa, ON: Canadian Hydrographic Service and University of New Brunswick Ocean Mapping Group.
  * *Core Error Propagation Contribution:* Provides the definitive mathematical derivation of the multibeam Total Propagated Uncertainty (TPU) Jacobian matrix, systematically expanding the partial derivatives of vessel position, motion sensor roll/pitch/heave, gyro heading, transducer alignment angles, acoustic travel times, and sound speed profiles.
  * *Engineering & DEM Relevance:* Forms the foundational mathematical error model embedded in hydrographic processing engines to compute $95\%$ confidence bounds and certify survey compliance against international standards.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 10, Chapter 14.

* **Harris, C. R., Millman, K. J., van der Walt, S. J., Gommers, R., Virtanen, P., Cournapeau, D., Wieser, E., Taylor, J., et al.** (2020). *Array programming with NumPy.* *Nature*, 585(7825), 357-362. DOI: [10.1038/s41586-020-2649-2](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-020-2649-2).
  * Definitive reference on `ndarray` vectorized memory buffers underlying scientific Python elevation pipelines.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 34.

* **Hartley, R., & Zisserman, A.** (2004). *Multiple View Geometry in Computer Vision.* *Cambridge University Press*. DOI: [10.1017/CBO9780511811685](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511811685).
  * **Mathematical & Geometric Synthesis:** Serves as the definitive mathematical reference for projective geometry in $\mathbb{P}^2$ and $\mathbb{P}^3$, deriving the camera projection matrix $\mathbf{P} = \mathbf{K}[\mathbf{R} \mid \mathbf{t}]$, the uncalibrated Fundamental matrix ($\mathbf{x}'^{\mathsf{T}}\mathbf{F}\mathbf{x} = 0$), the calibrated Essential matrix ($\mathbf{E} = [\mathbf{t}]_\times \mathbf{R}$), planar homographies, and trifocal tensors.
  * **DEM Generation & Error Significance:** Its rigorous derivations of isotropic coordinate normalization (pre-conditioning the 8-point algorithm), optimal $L_2$ polynomial triangulation (avoiding non-projectively invariant midpoint skew errors), and projective-to-metric gauge upgrades prevent conditioning blunders in ray intersection.
  * **Operational & Software Implementation:** Directly underpins the epipolar rectification, relative pose estimation, and two-view/multi-view triangulation kernels of `OpenCV`, `COLMAP`, `AliceVision`, and `NASA ASP`.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 8.

DOI: [10.1145/2369220.2369236](https://doi.org/10.1145/2369220.2369236).

* **Hawker, L., Uhe, P., Paulo, L., Sosa, J., Savage, J., Sampson, C., & Neal, J.** (2022). *A 30 m global map of elevation with forests and buildings removed.* *Environmental Research Letters*, 17(2), 024016. DOI: [10.1088/1748-9326/ac4d4f](https://doi.org/10.1088/1748-9326/ac4d4f).
  * *Demonstrates global spaceborne DSM-to-DTM conversion by training Random Forest canopy and building height models on NASA GEDI and ICESat-2 laser altimetry over Copernicus GLO-30.*

* **Hawley, J. H.** (1928). *Hydrographic Manual* (U.S. Coast and Geodetic Survey Special Publication No. 143). Washington, D.C.: U.S. Government Printing Office.
  * *The landmark historical manual that first standardized orthogonal cross-lines, systematic sounding line spacing, and lead-line calibration protocols in the United States.*

* **Hays, J. D., & Pitman, W. C.** (1973). *Lithospheric Plate Motion, Sea Level Changes and Climatic and Ecological Consequences.* *Nature*, 246(5427), 18-22. DOI: [10.1038/246018a0](https://doi.org/10.1038/246018a0).
  * Referenced in Section 04.03 (Chapter 4) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.

* **Heezen, B. C., & Ewing, W. M.** (1952). *Turbidity currents and submarine slumps, and the 1929 Grand Banks [Newfoundland] earthquake.* *American Journal of Science*, 250(12), 849-873. DOI: [10.2475/ajs.250.12.849](https://doi.org/10.2475/ajs.250.12.849).
  * **Scope:** Submarine Geohazards, Turbidity Currents, and Subsea Cable Engineering.
  * **Critical Annotation:** By analyzing the exact timestamps and spatial positions of 12 transatlantic telegraph cables that snapped sequentially down the Laurentian Fan over 13 hours and 17 minutes following the $M_w = 7.2$ Grand Banks earthquake of November 18, 1929, Heezen and Ewing proved the existence of high-velocity ($>14\text{--}25\text{ m s}^{-1}$), catastrophic submarine turbidity currents. This paper overturned the prevailing view of the deep seafloor as a quiescent environment and demonstrated how continental slope morphology ($S = \tan\beta$) funnels sediment-laden density flows across hundreds of kilometers into the abyssal plain.
  * **Practitioner Insight:** Remains the foundational case study for subsea telecommunications cable and pipeline routing engineers using high-resolution bathymetric DEMs to avoid submarine canyon chutes, steep continental slope gradients, and active fault scarps.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 1, Chapter 22, Chapter 29.

* **Heezen, B. C., Tharp, M., & Ewing, M.** (1959). *The Floors of the Oceans.* *Geological Society of America Special Papers*, 1-126. DOI: [10.1130/SPE65-p1](https://doi.org/10.1130/SPE65-p1).
  * **Scope:** *Accompanies the 1957 Heezen–Tharp Physiographic Diagram of the North Atlantic (compiled beginning in 1952) and culminates in Heezen, B. C., & Tharp, M. (1977), World Ocean Floor Panorama, Office of Naval Research / U.S. Navy.*
  * **Critical Annotation:** Synthesizing hundreds of thousands of discrete single-beam precision depth recorder (PDR) profiles into continuous three-dimensional physiographic diagrams, Marie Tharp and Bruce Heezen revealed the continuous $65,000\text{ km}$ mid-ocean ridge system and its central v-shaped axial rift valley. Tharp's meticulous profile-to-profile interpolation—bridging vast inter-track data gaps through geomorphological pattern recognition and earthquake epicenter overlay—provided pivotal observational evidence for seafloor spreading and plate tectonics.
  * **Practitioner Insight:** Represents the historical bridge between sparse 1D profile soundings and continuous 2D/3D global bathymetric models, illustrating both the scientific power and the epistemic assumptions of physically informed spatial interpolation.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 1.

* **Heidemann, H. K.** (2012; rev. 2018–2024). *Lidar base specification.* *Techniques and Methods*. DOI: [10.3133/tm11B4](https://doi.org/10.3133/tm11B4).
  * **Scope:** National 3D Elevation Program (3DEP) Lidar & DEM Standard.
  * **Critical Annotation:** This specification governs the acquisition, classification, vertical accuracy testing, and raster DEM generation for the USGS 3D Elevation Program (3DEP) across the United States. It defines Quality Levels by Aggregate Nominal Pulse Density ($\text{ANPD}$), Aggregate Nominal Pulse Spacing ($\text{ANPS}$), and vertical accuracy: **QL0** ($\text{RMSE}_z \le 5.0\text{ cm}, \text{NVA} \le 9.8\text{ cm}, \text{VVA} \le 15.0\text{ cm}, \text{ANPD} \ge 8.0\text{ pulses/m}^2$), **QL1** ($\text{RMSE}_z \le 10.0\text{ cm}, \text{NVA} \le 19.6\text{ cm}, \text{VVA} \le 30.0\text{ cm}, \text{ANPD} \ge 8.0\text{ pulses/m}^2, \text{ANPS} \le 0.35\text{ m}$), and **QL2** ($\text{RMSE}_z \le 10.0\text{ cm}, \text{NVA} \le 19.6\text{ cm}, \text{VVA} \le 30.0\text{ cm}, \text{ANPD} \ge 2.0\text{ pulses/m}^2, \text{ANPS} \le 0.71\text{ m}$), alongside strict rules for waterbody hydro-flattening and monotonic river breakline enforcement.
  * **Practitioner Insight:** Used both to write airborne lidar acquisition contracts and to understand the exact semantic properties (e.g., bridge decks removed from bare-earth class 2, but culverts left unbreached unless specifically hydro-enforced) of standard 3DEP DEMs.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 1, Chapter 5, Chapter 9, Chapter 13, Chapter 17, Chapter 24.

* **Hell, B., & Jakobsson, M.** (2011). *Gridding heterogeneous bathymetric data sets with stacked continuous curvature splines in tension.* *Marine Geophysical Research*, 32(4), 493-501. DOI: [10.1007/s11001-011-9141-1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11001-011-9141-1).
  * *Demonstrates hierarchical multi-resolution spline-in-tension stacking for combining dense multibeam corridors with sparse single-beam and historical lead-line soundings without aliasing.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 23.

* **Hengl, T.** (2006). *Finding the right pixel size.* *Computers & Geosciences*, 32(9), 1283-1298. DOI: [10.1016/j.cageo.2005.11.008](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cageo.2005.11.008).
  * *Establishes objective mathematical formulas linking optimal raster pixel size to point density, contour spacing, positioning uncertainty, and semivariogram range.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 18.

* **Hess, K. W., White, S. A., Sellars, J., Spargo, E. A., Wong, A., Gill, S. K., & Zervas, C. E.** (2004). *North Carolina Sea Level Rise Project: Interim Technical Report*. NOAA Technical Memorandum NOS CS 5 (paired with **NOAA, 2012**, *VDatum Manual for Development and Support of NOAA's Vertical Datum Transformation Tool, VDatum, Version 1.01*, NOAA Technical Memorandum NOS CS 29, and **Hess, K. W., Jeong, I., & White, S. A., 2012**, *Revised VDatum for Eastern Florida*, NOAA Technical Memorandum NOS CS 30). Silver Spring, MD: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration.
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** Documents the mathematical and grid architecture of NOAA's `VDatum` software and its application to seamless coastal topobathymetric DEMs. Details how two-dimensional finite-element hydrodynamic models (`ADCIRC`) are assimilated with coastal tide-gauge observations via spatially interpolated correction fields (`TCARI`) to generate continuous marine grids of tidal datums relative to LMSL, and how the Topography of the Sea Surface ($\text{TSS} = H_{\text{LMSL}}$, the orthometric height of the LMSL surface relative to NAVD88; stored with opposite sign in VDatum's internal `tss.gtx` grid as the height of NAVD88 zero relative to LMSL) links tidal datums to orthometric and ellipsoidal frames.
  * **Validation and Error Budget:** Establishes the root-sum-square (RSS) cumulative uncertainty propagation formula across the three-tier transformation chain ($\text{Ellipsoid} \leftrightarrow \text{Orthometric} \leftrightarrow \text{LMSL} \leftrightarrow \text{Tidal Datum}$):
    $$\sigma_{\text{total}} = \sqrt{\sigma_{\text{source}}^2 + \sigma_{\text{ellip}\leftrightarrow\text{ortho}}^2 + \sigma_{\text{TSS}}^2 + \sigma_{\text{LMSL}\leftrightarrow\text{tidal}}^2}$$
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** Provides the blueprint for seamless topobathymetric DEM assembly, demonstrating how omission of the VDatum transformation creates artificial $0.5\text{–}3.0\text{ m}$ vertical cliffs along the land–sea interface.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 3.

* **Hesse, R.** (2010). *LiDAR‐derived Local Relief Models – a new tool for archaeological prospection.* *Archaeological Prospection*, 17(2), 67-72. DOI: [10.1002/arp.374](https://doi.org/10.1002/arp.374).
  * Originates the Local Relief Model (LRM) methodology, subtracting low-pass trend surfaces from high-resolution DEMs to reveal subtle micro-topography.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 25.

* **Heuvelink, G. B. M.** (1998). *Error Propagation in Environmental Modelling with GIS.* *CRC Press*. DOI: [10.4324/9780203016114](https://doi.org/10.4324/9780203016114).
  * Foundational monograph unifying Taylor-series analytical variance propagation and conditional geostatistical Monte Carlo simulation for non-linear GIS terrain and environmental models.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 33.

* **Hickman, G. D., & Hogg, J. E.** (1969). *Application of an airborne pulsed laser for near shore bathymetric measurements.* *Remote Sensing of Environment*, 1(1), 47-58. DOI: [10.1016/S0034-4257(69)90088-1](https://doi.org/10.1016/S0034-4257(69)90088-1).
  * Referenced in Section 09.04 (Chapter 9) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 9.

* **Hilbert, D.** (1891). *Ueber die stetige Abbildung einer Line auf ein Fl�chenst�ck.* *Mathematische Annalen*, 38(3), 459-460. DOI: [10.1007/BF01199431](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01199431).
  * *The landmark publication introducing the continuous 2D Hilbert space-filling curve via recursive geometric quadrant reflections and rotations.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 16.

* **Hirschmuller, H.** (2008). *Stereo Processing by Semiglobal Matching and Mutual Information.* *IEEE Transactions on Pattern Analysis and Machine Intelligence*, 30(2), 328-341. DOI: [10.1109/TPAMI.2007.1166](https://doi.org/10.1109/TPAMI.2007.1166).
  * **Mathematical & Geometric Synthesis:** Introduces **Semi-Global Matching (`SGM`)**, which approximates NP-hard 2D Markov Random Field disparity optimization in $\mathcal{O}(WH D)$ complexity by aggregating 1D dynamic-programming smoothness penalties ($P_1$ for $|\Delta d| = 1$ slanted surfaces; $P_2 \ge P_1$ for $|\Delta d| > 1$ depth discontinuities) radially along 8 or 16 image paths.
  * **DEM Generation & Error Significance:** Combined with Census or Mutual Information matching costs and parabolic/equiangular sub-pixel refinement, `SGM` became the workhorse dense matcher for airborne, UAV, and satellite DSM production—though its discrete integer disparity preference can produce characteristic contour "terracing" (stair-stepping) on gentle topographic slopes when $P_1$ is over-weighted or parabolic interpolation exhibits pixel-locking.
  * **Operational & Software Implementation:** Implemented in `OpenCV` (`StereoSGBM`), `libSGM`, `ASP` (`--stereo-algorithm asp_sgm`), `MicMac`, and commercial suites (`SURE`, `Inpho`).
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 8.

* **Hladik, C., & Alber, M.** (2012). *Accuracy assessment and correction of a LIDAR-derived salt marsh digital elevation model.* *Remote Sensing of Environment*, 121, 224-235. DOI: [10.1016/j.rse.2012.01.018](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.rse.2012.01.018).
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** Quantifies the systematic positive elevation bias ($\Delta z_{\text{veg}} = +0.05\text{ m}$ in short *Spartina alterniflora* and *Salicornia virginica* to $+0.25\text{–}0.65\text{ m}$ in dense *Juncus roemerianus* and tall creekbank *Spartina*) present in standard "bare-earth" airborne topographic LiDAR DEMs of intertidal salt marshes due to laser pulse backscatter within dense halophytic canopies.
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** Demonstrates that even after rigorous geoid and VDatum transformations, an uncorrected coastal LiDAR DEM will severely underestimate tidal prism volume and misplace the MHW/MHHW contours across low-gradient marshes ($\tan\beta \le 0.002$) unless species-specific RTK-GNSS bias corrections or multispectral NDVI/biomass elevation-adjustment models (such as `LEAN`) are applied prior to topobathymetric fusion.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 3.

* **Hobby, J. D.** (1999). *Practical segment intersection with finite precision output.* *Computational Geometry*, 13(4), 199-214. DOI: [10.1016/S0925-7721(99)00021-8](https://doi.org/10.1016/S0925-7721(99)00021-8).
  * *Formulates the hot-pixel snap-rounding algorithm used in JTS and GEOS to guarantee topologically valid, self-intersection-free vector overlays on fixed-precision grids.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 17.

* **Hodúl, M., Bird, S., Knudby, A., & Chénier, R.** (2018). *Satellite derived photogrammetric bathymetry.* *ISPRS Journal of Photogrammetry and Remote Sensing*, 142, 268-277. DOI: [10.1016/j.isprsjprs.2018.06.015](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.isprsjprs.2018.06.015).
  * Referenced in Section 07.05 (Chapter 7) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 7.

* **Höfle, B., & Pfeifer, N.** (2007). *Correction of laser scanning intensity data: Data and model-driven approaches.* *ISPRS Journal of Photogrammetry and Remote Sensing*, 62(6), 415-433. DOI: [10.1016/j.isprsjprs.2007.05.008](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.isprsjprs.2007.05.008).
  * Derives the laser radar range equation applied to airborne laser scanning, proving that combining true 3D surface slope angles with distance normalization isolates true physical target reflectance.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 28.

* **Hofmann-Wellenhof, B., & Moritz, H.** (2006). *Physical Geodesy.* *Springer Vienna*. DOI: [10.1007/978-3-211-33545-1](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-211-33545-1).
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** The definitive graduate-level treatise on gravitational potential theory and physical height systems, updating Heiskanen and Moritz's classic 1967 monograph. Derives Laplace's and Poisson's equations, the normal earth gravity field $U$ and normal gravity $\gamma$, the disturbing potential $T = W - U$, Bruns's formula for the geoid undulation ($N = T_0/\gamma_0$, with $T_0$ on the geoid and normal gravity $\gamma_0$ on the reference ellipsoid) and Molodensky's height anomaly ($\zeta = T_P/\gamma_Q$, with $T_P$ at the physical surface and $\gamma_Q$ on the telluroid), Stokes's boundary-value integral, and Molodensky's surface free-air boundary problem from first principles.
  * **Key Formulation and Chapter Pointers:** Chapter 4 (Sections 4.1–4.6) rigorously contrasts path-independent geopotential numbers ($C_P = W_0 - W_P = \int_0^P g\,dn$) against physical height realizations:
    $$H = \frac{C_P}{\bar{g}} \quad \text{(Helmert orthometric)}, \qquad H^* = \frac{C_P}{\bar{\gamma}} \quad \text{(Molodensky normal)}$$
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** Essential for understanding why raw spirit-leveled height differences ($\Delta n$) are non-integrable around closed loops unless converted to geopotential differences via surface gravity $g$, and why national vertical datums split between geoid-referenced orthometric systems (USA, Canada) and quasigeoid-referenced normal height systems (Europe's EVRF, Russia, China).
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 2, Chapter 3.

* **Hofmann-Wellenhof, B., Lichtenegger, H., & Wasle, E.** (2008). *GNSS — Global Navigation Satellite Systems.* *Springer Vienna*. DOI: [10.1007/978-3-211-73017-1](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-211-73017-1).
  * Referenced in Section 05.02 (Chapter 5) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 5.

* **Hoge, F. E., Swift, R. N., & Frederick, E. B.** (1980). *Water depth measurement using an airborne pulsed neon laser system.* *Applied Optics*, 19(6), 871. DOI: [10.1364/AO.19.000871](https://doi.org/10.1364/AO.19.000871).
  * Referenced in Section 09.04 (Chapter 9) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 9.

* **Höhle, J., & Höhle, M.** (2009). *Accuracy assessment of digital elevation models by means of robust statistical methods.* *ISPRS Journal of Photogrammetry and Remote Sensing*, 64(4), 398-406. DOI: [10.1016/j.isprsjprs.2009.02.003](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.isprsjprs.2009.02.003).
  * *Introduced robust non-parametric estimators to elevation model validation, establishing the Normalized Median Absolute Deviation (NMAD) and empirical quantile testing as standard practices.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 14.

* **Hoitink, A. J. F., & Jay, D. A.** (2016). *Tidal river dynamics: Implications for deltas.* *Reviews of Geophysics*, 54(1), 240-272. DOI: [10.1002/2015RG000507](https://doi.org/10.1002/2015RG000507).
  * Referenced in Section 03.02 (Chapter 3) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 3.

* **Holman, R. A., & Stanley, J.** (2007). *The history and technical capabilities of Argus.* *Coastal Engineering*, 54(6-7), 477-491. DOI: [10.1016/j.coastaleng.2007.01.003](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.coastaleng.2007.01.003).
  * Referenced in Section 07.06 (Chapter 7) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 7.

* **Holman, R., & Haller, M. C.** (2013). *Remote Sensing of the Nearshore.* *Annual Review of Marine Science*, 5(1), 95-113. DOI: [10.1146/annurev-marine-121211-172408](https://doi.org/10.1146/annurev-marine-121211-172408).
  * **Platform & Kinematic Focus:** Synthesizes the physics and operational deployment of stationary coastal remote sensing platforms—predominantly tower/hotel-mounted optical video stations (`Argus`, see also Holman & Stanley, 2007, *Coastal Engineering*, 54, 477–491) and marine X-band grazing-incidence radars.
  * **DEM Uncertainty & Error Budget Implications:** Shows how fixed platforms overcome the prohibitive vessel hazards and optical turbidity of the high-energy surf zone by observing temporal wave kinematics continuously across storms, linking time-exposure (`timex`) foam dissipation and wave phase propagation directly to intertidal and subtidal morphology.
  * **Validation & Operational Guidance:** Compares optical video and marine X-band radar footprint scaling, grazing-angle shadowing, and extrinsic camera-orientation stability requirements against amphibious surf-zone truth surveys.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 7.

* **Holman, R., Plant, N., & Holland, T.** (2013). *cBathy: A robust algorithm for estimating nearshore bathymetry.* *Journal of Geophysical Research: Oceans*, 118(5), 2595-2609. DOI: [10.1002/jgrc.20199](https://doi.org/10.1002/jgrc.20199).
  * **Platform & Kinematic Focus:** Formulates the standard spectral wave-inversion pipeline (`cBathy`) for fixed coastal camera arrays and X-band marine radars, using cross-spectral Complex Empirical Orthogonal Function (CEOF) analysis of pixel intensity time series to extract dominant wave frequencies ($\omega$) and spatial wavenumbers ($k$).
  * **DEM Uncertainty & Error Budget Implications:** Inverts the linear intermediate-water dispersion relation ($\omega^2 = gk\tanh(kh) \implies h = \frac{1}{k}\tanh^{-1}\!\left(\frac{\omega^2}{gk}\right)$) for water depth $h$ and assimilates consecutive estimates via a Kalman filter weighted by phase skill, while rigorously quantifying the $20\%\text{–}40\%$ positive depth bias inside the breaking surf zone where non-linear amplitude dispersion increases wave speed.
  * **Validation & Operational Guidance:** Benchmarked against amphibious CRAB and personal-watercraft echo-sounder surveys at the USACE Field Research Facility (Duck, NC); open-sourced via the Coastal Imaging Research Network (`CIRN`).
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 7.

* **Holmes, K. W., Chadwick, O. A., & Kyriakidis, P. C.** (2000). *Error in a USGS 30-meter digital elevation model and its impact on terrain modeling.* *Journal of Hydrology*, 233(1-4), 154-173. DOI: [10.1016/S0022-1694(00)00229-8](https://doi.org/10.1016/S0022-1694(00)00229-8).
  * Classic empirical study combining differential GPS checkpoints with conditional Sequential Gaussian Simulation (`sgsim`) to quantify non-linear upslope contributing area uncertainty.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 33.

* **Holynski, A., & Kopf, J.** (2018). *Fast depth densification for occlusion-aware augmented reality.* *ACM Transactions on Graphics*, 37(6), 1-11. DOI: [10.1145/3272127.3275083](https://doi.org/10.1145/3272127.3275083).
  * Derives real-time edge-aware depth densification from sparse SLAM/VIO features to enforce crisp foreground-background occlusion in handheld mobile AR.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 35.

* **Horn, B. K. P.** (1975). Obtaining shape from shading information. In P. H. Winston (Ed.), *The Psychology of Computer Vision* (pp. 115–155). New York: McGraw-Hill.
  * *The pioneering formulation of Shape-from-Shading (photoclinometry), deriving the non-linear reflectance map equation relating optical radiance to physical surface gradients.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 18.

* **Horn, B. K. P.** (1981). *Hill shading and the reflectance map.* *Proceedings of the IEEE*, 69(1), 14-47. DOI: [10.1109/PROC.1981.11918](https://doi.org/10.1109/PROC.1981.11918).
  * Foundational paper deriving analytical shaded relief, surface normal vectors, and the distance-weighted $3\times 3$ finite-difference gradient operator used in almost all GIS software today.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 25, Chapter 34.

* **Horn, B. K. P.** (1990). *Height and gradient from shading.* *International Journal of Computer Vision*, 5(1), 37-75. DOI: [10.1007/BF00056771](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF00056771).
  * *Derives the variational Euler-Lagrange PDE for simultaneous height and gradient reconstruction from shading with integrability and smoothness constraints.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 18.

* **Hostetler, L., & Andreas, R.** (1983). *Nonlinear Kalman filtering techniques for terrain-aided navigation.* *IEEE Transactions on Automatic Control*, 28(3), 315-323. DOI: [10.1109/TAC.1983.1103232](https://doi.org/10.1109/TAC.1983.1103232).
  * Landmark Sandia National Laboratories paper establishing recursive Bayesian/SITAN terrain-aided navigation matching radar altimeter profiles against onboard DEM matrices.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 26.

* **Howat, I. M., Porter, C., Smith, B. E., Noh, M. J., & Morin, P.** (2019). *The Reference Elevation Model of Antarctica.* *The Cryosphere*, 13(2), 665-674. DOI: [10.5194/tc-13-665-2019](https://doi.org/10.5194/tc-13-665-2019).
  * Documents the creation of REMA, detailing petascale stereophotogrammetry from WorldView imagery, SETSM processing, and ICESat laser altimetry co-registration.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 24, Chapter 30.

* **Hoyer, S., & Hamman, J.** (2017). *xarray: N-D labeled Arrays and Datasets in Python.* *Journal of Open Research Software*, 5(1), 10. DOI: [10.5334/jors.148](https://doi.org/10.5334/jors.148).
  * The architectural reference for `xarray`, establishing the labeled N-dimensional array model that powers the modern Pangeo scientific Python ecosystem.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 34.

* **Hu, Q., Yang, B., Xie, L., Rosa, S., Guo, Y., Wang, Z., Trigoni, N., & Markham, A.** (2020). *RandLA-Net: Efficient Semantic Segmentation of Large-Scale Point Clouds.* *2020 IEEE/CVF Conference on Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (CVPR)*, 11105-11114. DOI: [10.1109/CVPR42600.2020.01112](https://doi.org/10.1109/CVPR42600.2020.01112).
  * *Introduces random point sampling with dilated local feature aggregation, scaling direct 3D neural semantic segmentation to million-point LiDAR tiles.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 21.

* **Huang, B., Yu, Z., Chen, A., Geiger, A., & Gao, S.** (2024). *2D Gaussian Splatting for Geometrically Accurate Radiance Fields.* *Special Interest Group on Computer Graphics and Interactive Techniques Conference Conference Papers*, 1-11. DOI: [10.1145/3641519.3657428](https://doi.org/10.1145/3641519.3657428).
  * Extends Gaussian splatting to 2D surfels, enforcing geometric ray-splat consistency and enabling rigorous surface mesh and DEM extraction.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 25.

* **Hughes Clarke, J. E.** (2003). Dynamic motion residuals in swath sonar data: Ironing out the creases. *International Hydrographic Review*, 4(1), 6–23.
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** Hare (1995) derives the complete first-order Jacobian variance-propagation equations for **Multibeam Echosounder (`MBES`)** Total Vertical Uncertainty (`TVU`) and Total Horizontal Uncertainty (`THU`), coupling roll ($\phi$), pitch ($\theta$), yaw ($\psi$), heave ($h_{\text{heave}}$), sound-speed refraction ($\bar{c}$), and slant-range/beam-angle errors ($r, \beta$) across swath steering angle $\beta$:
    $$\sigma_z^2 \approx \sigma_{\text{heave}}^2 + \sigma_r^2\cos^2\beta + d^2\tan^2\beta\,\bigl(\sigma_\phi^2 + \sigma_\beta^2 + \dot{\phi}^2\sigma_{\delta t}^2\bigr) + d^2\tan^2\theta\,\sigma_\theta^2 + \frac{d^2\tan^4\beta}{4 c_0^2}\sigma_{\bar{c}}^2$$
    Hughes Clarke (2003) isolates dynamic motion and time-synchronization latency residuals ($\delta t_{\text{latency}}$) between the IMU and sonar ping epoch on a rolling vessel ($\phi(t) = \Phi_0 \sin(\omega_r t)$ with roll rate $\dot{\phi} = \frac{d\phi}{dt}$):
    $$\delta z_{\text{roll}}(y, t) \approx y \bigl[\delta\phi(t) + \dot{\phi}(t)\,\delta t_{\text{latency}}\bigr] = d \tan\beta \bigl[\delta\phi(t) + \dot{\phi}(t)\,\delta t_{\text{latency}}\bigr]$$
    where $y = d \tan\beta$ is across-track distance from nadir (in $\text{m}$) at water depth $d$ (in $\text{m}$, positive downward).
  * **Validation and Error Budget:** Proves that even a $5\text{ ms}$ uncalibrated time latency on a vessel rolling at $\dot{\phi} = 10^\circ\text{ s}^{-1}$ ($0.1745\text{ rad s}^{-1}$) induces an effective roll error of $0.05^\circ$, creating periodic $\pm 15\text{ cm}$ "washboard" outer-beam artifacts at $d = 100\text{ m}$ ($\beta = 60^\circ$), and codifies the mathematical basis of the hydrographic **patch test** (latency, roll, pitch, and yaw calibration lines) mandated by the `NOAA Hydrographic Surveys Specifications and Deliverables (HSSD)` and `NOAA Field Procedures Manual (FPM)`.
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** Directly governs the `CUBE` (`Combined Uncertainty and Bathymetric Estimator`) soundings-weighting algorithm, `IHO S-100`/`S-102` bathymetric surface uncertainty grids, and `IHO S-44` (6th ed., 2020) 95% confidence compliance ($\text{TVU}_{\max}(d) = \sqrt{a^2 + (b\cdot d)^2}$, with Exclusive Order $a = 0.15\text{ m}, b = 0.0075$, Special Order $a = 0.25\text{ m}, b = 0.0075$, and Order 1a/1b $a = 0.50\text{ m}, b = 0.013$) in `CARIS HIPS and SIPS`, `Qimera`, and `MB-System`.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 5, Chapter 7, Chapter 13.

* **Hughes Clarke, J. E.** (2018). *Multibeam Echosounders.* *Springer Geology*, 25-41. DOI: [10.1007/978-3-319-57852-1_3](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-319-57852-1_3).
  * *Core Methodological Contribution:* Provides an authoritative synthesis of modern multibeam system design written specifically for geomorphologists and hydrographers. Details the operational evolution from single-frequency CW systems to multi-sector FM chirp arrays, motion-stabilized beam steering, and real-time seafloor backscatter extraction.
  * *Engineering & DEM Relevance:* Dissects how platform dynamics (dynamic draft, vessel settlement, roll/pitch/yaw misalignments) and water-column oceanography interact to create non-geological artifacts in high-resolution digital elevation models.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 10.

* **Hughes Clarke, J. E., Mayer, L. A., & Wells, D. E.** (1996). *Shallow-water imaging multibeam sonars: A new tool for investigating seafloor processes in the coastal zone and on the continental shelf.* *Marine Geophysical Researches*, 18(6), 607-629. DOI: [10.1007/BF00313877](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF00313877).
  * Foundational paper demonstrating how co-registered high-resolution multibeam bathymetry and acoustic backscatter resolve continental-shelf bedforms and sediment transport processes.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 28.

* **Hugonnet, R., Brun, F., Berthier, E., Dehecq, A., Mannerfelt, E. S., Eckert, N., & Farinotti, D.** (2022). *Uncertainty Analysis of Digital Elevation Models by Spatial Inference From Stable Terrain.* *IEEE Journal of Selected Topics in Applied Earth Observations and Remote Sensing*, 15, 6456-6472. DOI: [10.1109/JSTARS.2022.3188922](https://doi.org/10.1109/JSTARS.2022.3188922).
  * *Establishes the `xdem` statistical framework for inferring slope/curvature-dependent heteroscedastic DEM errors and integrating multi-range variograms into exact spatially correlated volumetric uncertainty bounds.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 14, Chapter 22, Chapter 33.

* **Hugonnet, R., McNabb, R., Berthier, E., Menounos, B., Nuth, C., Girod, L., Farinotti, D., Huss, M., et al.** (2021). *Accelerated global glacier mass loss in the early twenty-first century.* *Nature*, 592(7856), 726-731. DOI: [10.1038/s41586-021-03436-z](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-021-03436-z).
  * Global multi-temporal stereo DEM analysis (`xdem` framework) quantifying the elevation change and mass balance of all Earth's mountain glaciers with spatially correlated error propagation.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 30, Chapter 33.

* **Hunter, G. J., & Goodchild, M. F.** (1997). *Modeling the Uncertainty of Slope and Aspect Estimates Derived from Spatial Databases.* *Geographical Analysis*, 29(1), 35-49. DOI: [10.1111/j.1538-4632.1997.tb00944.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1538-4632.1997.tb00944.x).
  * Foundational paper analyzing the coupled transfer of spatially autocorrelated elevation errors into derivative slope and aspect surfaces.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 33.

* **Hutchinson, M. F.** (1989). *A new procedure for gridding elevation and stream line data with automatic removal of spurious pits.* *Journal of Hydrology*, 106(3-4), 211-232. DOI: [10.1016/0022-1694(89)90073-5](https://doi.org/10.1016/0022-1694(89)90073-5).
  * *The seminal paper deriving the ANUDEM algorithm, introducing finite-difference multigrid relaxation with active stream monotonicity penalties and automated spurious sink clearance.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 17.

### I

* **Imhof, E.** (1982/2007). *Cartographic Relief Presentation* (H. J. Steward, Ed.; 1982 English ed. Walter de Gruyter; 2007 classic facsimile ed. Esri Press, Redlands, CA). ISBN: 978-1589480261.
  * The undisputed foundational masterwork on manual and theoretical topographic relief cartography. Covers contour interval selection, index line weighting, rock drawing, hachuring, and aerial perspective.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 25, Chapter 26.

* **Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers [IEEE Std 1588-2019]** (2020). *IEEE Standard for a Precision Clock Synchronization Protocol for Networked Measurement and Control Systems.* *IEEE*. DOI: [10.1109/IEEESTD.2020.9120376](https://doi.org/10.1109/IEEESTD.2020.9120376).
  * The authoritative engineering specification for Precision Time Protocol (PTP), codifying hardware physical-layer (PHY) timestamping, transparent clocks, and White Rabbit sub-nanosecond profiles.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 32.

* **Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers [IEEE Std 738-2012]** (2013/2014). *IEEE Standard for Calculating the Current-Temperature Relationship of Bare Overhead Conductors.* *IEEE*. DOI: [10.1109/IEEESTD.2013.6692858](https://doi.org/10.1109/IEEESTD.2013.6692858).
  * *Specifies the governing convective, radiative, solar, and Joule ($I^2 R$) heat-balance equations linking electrical dispatch and weather to conductor core temperature.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 20.

* **Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers [IEEE Std 952-1997]** (1998; reaffirmed 2008). *IEEE Standard Specification Format Guide and Test Procedure for Single-Axis Interferometric Fiber Optic Gyros.* *IEEE*. DOI: [10.1109/IEEESTD.1998.86153](https://doi.org/10.1109/IEEESTD.1998.86153).
  * Referenced in Section 05.03 (Chapter 5) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 5.

* **International Hydrographic Organization [IHO B-12]** (2022). *Guidance on Crowdsourced Bathymetry* (Publication B-12, Edition 3.0.0). Monaco: International Hydrographic Organization.
  * Authoritative technical and policy guidance for collecting, validating, and submitting opportunistic depth data from commercial ships and citizen mariners to the IHO DCDB.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 27.

* **International Hydrographic Organization [IHO S-102]** (2022/2023). *S-102 Bathymetric Surface Product Specification* (Edition 2.1.0 / 2.2.0). Monaco: International Hydrographic Organization.
  * Formal geospatial standard defining high-resolution gridded bathymetric deliverables (positive-up HDF5 BAG structure) and dynamic safety-depth contour rendering inside next-generation Electronic Chart Display and Information Systems (ECDIS).
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 15, Chapter 19, Chapter 26.

* **International Hydrographic Organization [IHO S-44]** (2020/2022). *IHO Standards for Hydrographic Surveys* (Special Publication No. 44, 6th Edition, 2020; Edition 6.1.0, 2022). Monaco: International Hydrographic Organization.
  * **Scope:** IHO Publications S-44, S-100, and S-102 | Global Safety-of-Navigation Survey & Gridded Surface Standards.
  * **Critical Annotation:** IHO S-44 is the global authoritative standard governing hydrographic survey classification for safety of navigation across all maritime nations. It defines five survey orders—**Exclusive** ($a = 0.15\text{ m}, b = 0.0075, \text{THU}_{\max} = 1.0\text{ m}, 200\%$ coverage, $0.5\text{ m}$ feature cube), **Special** ($a = 0.25\text{ m}, b = 0.0075, \text{THU}_{\max} = 2.0\text{ m}, 100\%$ coverage, $1.0\text{ m}$ feature cube), **Order 1a** ($a = 0.50\text{ m}, b = 0.013, \text{THU}_{\max} = 5.0\text{ m} + 0.05d, 100\%$ coverage), **Order 1b** (same $\text{TVU}/\text{THU}$ as 1a, $5\%$ general coverage), and **Order 2** ($a = 1.00\text{ m}, b = 0.023, \text{THU}_{\max} = 20.0\text{ m} + 0.10d$)—specifying maximum allowable Total Vertical Uncertainty (at the 95% confidence level) via the depth-dependent formula $\text{TVU}_{\max}(d) = \sqrt{a^2 + (b \cdot d)^2}$. Companion specification **S-102** (built on the **S-100** HDF5 framework) standardizes the delivery of regular and variable-resolution bathymetric grids carrying paired depth and uncertainty layers.
  * **Practitioner Insight:** Every marine surveyor, nautical cartographer, and hydrographic software developer must master S-44 and S-102 to configure sensor error budgets, CUBE disambiguation parameters, and shoal-biased electronic navigational chart surfaces.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 1, Chapter 7, Chapter 9, Chapter 10, Chapter 13, Chapter 14, Chapter 23, Chapter 24.

* **International Telecommunication Union (ITU).** (2014). *Technical characteristics for an automatic identification system using time division multiple access in the VHF maritime mobile frequency band* (Recommendation ITU-R M.1371-5). Geneva: ITU.
  * *Authoritative international radio specification defining SOTDMA slot protocols, dynamic position reports (`Messages 1–3, 18`), and hull-dimension offsets (`Message 5`) for marine AIS.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 22.

* **Isenburg, M.** (2013). *LASzip.* *Photogrammetric Engineering & Remote Sensing*, 79(2), 209-217. DOI: [10.14358/PERS.79.2.209](https://doi.org/10.14358/PERS.79.2.209).
  * *Core Algorithmic Contribution:* Presents the streaming, predictive arithmetic coding algorithm behind `LAZ` (`LASzip`), which compresses standard ASPRS `LAS` binary point clouds to $7\%\text{–}20\%$ of their uncompressed size with zero loss of numerical precision or byte-order fidelity.
  * *Engineering & DEM Relevance:* By partitioning point streams into independent chunks (typically $50{,}000$ points) and exploiting spatiotemporal coherence along the scanner's trajectory via delta predictors for GPS time, quantized integer coordinates $(X_{\text{int}}, Y_{\text{int}}, Z_{\text{int}})$, scan angle, and return bit-flags, `LASzip` enabled petabyte-scale national LiDAR archives and laid the direct foundation for Cloud-Optimized Point Clouds (`COPC`).
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 9, Chapter 15, Chapter 19.

* **ISO & OGC [ISO 19111] — International Organization for Standardization** (2019). *ISO 19111:2019 Geographic information — Referencing by coordinates* (OGC Topic 2, Document 18-005r4). Geneva: ISO.
  * **Scope:** ISO 19111:2019 / OGC `18-005r4` | Conceptual Schema for Dynamic Geodetic Reference Frames and Datum Ensembles.
  * **Critical Annotation:** Developed jointly by ISO/TC 211 and the OGC CRS Standards Working Group, the third edition of ISO 19111 overhauled the international geospatial data model to accommodate four-dimensional space geodesy. It formally separates static crust-fixed datums from `DynamicGeodeticReferenceFrame` (requiring a frame reference epoch $t_0$ and velocity model) and introduces `DatumEnsemble` (explicitly modeling collections of realizations such as generic `EPSG:4326` WGS84 or `EPSG:6269` NAD83 with an ensemble positional inaccuracy bound).
  * **Key Formulations & Error Budgets:** Codifies the formal relationship between coordinate operations (`ConcatenatedOperation`, `PassThroughOperation`, `Transformation`, and `Conversion`) and mandates explicit `CoordinateMetadata` objects binding dynamic point clouds or DEM grids to an observation epoch $t$.
  * **Practitioner Insight:** Establishes the conceptual requirement—adopted across IHO S-100/S-102, USGS 3DEP, and ASPRS standards—that any high-precision elevation or bathymetric dataset referenced to a dynamic frame must carry its coordinate epoch $t$ alongside its CRS identifier.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 2.

* **ISO & OGC [ISO 19125-1] — International Organization for Standardization** (2011). *ISO 19125-1 Geographic information — Simple feature access — Part 1: Common architecture*. Geneva: ISO.
  * *The definitive open standard specifying vector geometry primitives (Point, LineString, Polygon, PolyhedralSurfaceZ, TINZ) and Well-Known Text (WKT) serializations.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 17.

* **ISO & OGC [ISO 19162] — International Organization for Standardization** (2019). *ISO 19162:2019 Geographic information — Well-known text representation of coordinate reference systems (WKT2:2019)* (OGC Document 18-010r7). Geneva: ISO.
  * **Scope:** ISO 19162:2019 (WKT2:2019) | Authoritative Text Serialization Standard for Coordinate Reference Systems and Operations.
  * **Critical Annotation:** Translates the ISO 19111:2019 conceptual model into machine-readable Well-Known Text version 2 (WKT2:2019). It defines unambiguous syntax for `DYNAMIC[FRAMEEPOCH[...]]`, `ENSEMBLE[...]`, `COMPOUNDCRS[...]`, explicit `CS` axis order and direction (`north`, `east`, `up`, `down`), and `COORDINATEOPERATION[...]` blocks that record exact transformation parameters and grid files.
  * **Key Formulations & Error Budgets:** Eliminates the unit, axis-order, and datum-realization ambiguities of legacy WKT1 (`OGC 01-009` / `99-049`) and `PROJ.4` strings by preserving explicit `USAGE[SCOPE[...], AREA[...], BBOX[...], TIMEEXTENT[...]]` and `ID["EPSG", ...]` nodes.
  * **Practitioner Insight:** Mastering WKT2:2019 is essential for preventing silent datum, vertical sign (`up` vs. `down`), and axis-order corruption across modern Cloud-Optimized GeoTIFF (COG), COPC, LAS 1.4, IHO S-102 HDF5, and GeoPackage pipelines.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 2, Chapter 34.

* **ISO & OGC [ISO 19170-1] — International Organization for Standardization** (2021). *ISO 19170-1:2021 Geographic information — Discrete Global Grid Systems Specifications — Part 1: Core Reference System and Operations, and Equal Area Earth Reference System*. Geneva: ISO.
  * *The international open standard establishing the formal abstract specification, terminology, and functional requirements for Discrete Global Grid Systems.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 16.

* **ISO [ISO 12233] — International Organization for Standardization** (2017). *ISO 12233:2017 Photography — Electronic still picture imaging — Resolution and spatial frequency responses*. Geneva: ISO.
  * *Defines the international standard slanted-edge methodology for extracting the Edge Spread Function (ESF), Line Spread Function (LSF), and Modulation Transfer Function (MTF).*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 12.

### J

* **Jackson, D. R., Winebrenner, D. P., & Ishimaru, A.** (1986). *Application of the composite roughness model to high-frequency bottom backscattering.* *The Journal of the Acoustical Society of America*, 79(5), 1410-1422. DOI: [10.1121/1.393669](https://doi.org/10.1121/1.393669).
  * Foundational acoustic physics paper deriving the composite roughness and volume scattering model (the Jackson model) used worldwide to invert backscatter curves into physical sediment grain size.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 28.

* **James, M. R., & Robson, S.** (2014). *Mitigating systematic error in topographic models derived from UAV and ground‐based image networks.* *Earth Surface Processes and Landforms*, 39(10), 1413-1420. DOI: [10.1002/esp.3609](https://doi.org/10.1002/esp.3609).
  * **Mathematical & Geometric Synthesis:** Demonstrates analytically and empirically that parallel, single-altitude nadir UAV flight blocks suffer from severe projective parameter coupling between interior radial lens distortion ($k_1$) and exterior camera station elevation ($Z_0$) during self-calibrating bundle adjustment.
  * **DEM Generation & Error Significance:** This correlation bends the reconstructed ground surface into a systematic quadratic **"dome" or "bowl" ($\Delta z(R) \propto \delta k_1 R^2$)** whose amplitude can exceed $10\text{–}50\times$ the random point noise; adding $15^\circ\text{–}20^\circ$ convergent oblique cross-strips or well-distributed 3D Ground Control Points breaks the collinear degeneracy and collapses systematic DEM doming below the centimeter level.
  * **Operational & Software Implementation:** Establishes the standard oblique cross-grid UAV survey protocol enforced in modern flight planners (`QGroundControl`, `UgCS`, `DJI Pilot`) and self-calibration diagnostics (`MicMac`, `ODM`).
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 7, Chapter 8, Chapter 12, Chapter 13.

* **Jay, D. A., & Flinchem, E. P.** (1997). *Interaction of fluctuating river flow with a barotropic tide: A demonstration of wavelet tidal analysis methods.* *Journal of Geophysical Research: Oceans*, 102(C3), 5705-5720. DOI: [10.1029/96JC00496](https://doi.org/10.1029/96JC00496).
  * Referenced in Section 03.02 (Chapter 3) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 3.

* **Jayne, S. R., & St. Laurent, L. C.** (2001). *Parameterizing tidal dissipation over rough topography.* *Geophysical Research Letters*, 28(5), 811-814. DOI: [10.1029/2000GL012044](https://doi.org/10.1029/2000GL012044).
  * Referenced in Section 01.02 (Chapter 1) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 1.

  * Referenced in Section 05.03 (Chapter 5) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 5.

* **Johnson-Roberson, M., Pizarro, O., Williams, S. B., & Mahon, I.** (2010). *Generation and visualization of large‐scale three‐dimensional reconstructions from underwater robotic surveys.* *Journal of Field Robotics*, 27(1), 21-51. DOI: [10.1002/rob.20324](https://doi.org/10.1002/rob.20324).
