### K

* **Kaess, M., Johannsson, H., Roberts, R., Ila, V., Leonard, J. J., & Dellaert, F.** (2012). *iSAM2: Incremental smoothing and mapping using the Bayes tree.* *The International Journal of Robotics Research*, 31(2), 216-235. DOI: [10.1177/0278364911430419](https://doi.org/10.1177/0278364911430419).
  * Referenced in Section 06.01 (Chapter 6) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 6.

* **Kalman, R. E.** (1960). *A New Approach to Linear Filtering and Prediction Problems.* *Journal of Basic Engineering*, 82(1), 35-45. DOI: [10.1115/1.3662552](https://doi.org/10.1115/1.3662552).
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** Kalman (1960) replaces Wiener frequency-domain filtering with the recursive minimum-variance state-space estimator for discrete linear dynamical systems $\mathbf{x}_{k+1} = \boldsymbol{\Phi}_k\mathbf{x}_k + \mathbf{w}_k$ with measurements $\mathbf{z}_k = \mathbf{H}_k\mathbf{x}_k + \mathbf{v}_k$. Rauch, Tung, & Striebel (1965) derive the optimal **fixed-interval backward smoother (RTS smoother)**, which sweeps backward from final epoch $N$ to $k = 1$ using only the forward-filtered state estimates $\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{k|k}, \hat{\mathbf{x}}_{k+1|k}$, state transition matrices $\boldsymbol{\Phi}_k$, and covariance matrices $\mathbf{P}_{k|k}, \mathbf{P}_{k+1|k}$ without reprocessing raw measurements:
    $$\mathbf{K}_k^s = \mathbf{P}_{k|k} \boldsymbol{\Phi}_k^T \mathbf{P}_{k+1|k}^{-1}, \qquad \hat{\mathbf{x}}_{k|N} = \hat{\mathbf{x}}_{k|k} + \mathbf{K}_k^s \bigl(\hat{\mathbf{x}}_{k+1|N} - \hat{\mathbf{x}}_{k+1|k}\bigr), \qquad \mathbf{P}_{k|N} = \mathbf{P}_{k|k} + \mathbf{K}_k^s \bigl(\mathbf{P}_{k+1|N} - \mathbf{P}_{k+1|k}\bigr) (\mathbf{K}_k^s)^T$$
  * **Validation and Error Budget:** Converts the sawtooth error growth of forward-only Kalman filtering during GNSS outages (where position error grows cubically with outage duration, $\delta p \propto \frac{1}{6} b_g g\,\Delta t^3$) into a smooth, bounded quadratic bridge clamped at both ends of the outage, cutting peak trajectory and attitude RMS errors by $50\text{–}80\%$.
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** The exact mathematical engine that generates the **Smoothed Best Estimate of Trajectory (`SBET`)** and its time-varying covariance (`SMRMSG`) in `POSPac MMS` and `Inertial Explorer`.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 5, Chapter 14.

* **Karney, C. F. F.** (2011). *Transverse Mercator with an accuracy of a few nanometers.* *Journal of Geodesy*, 85(8), 475-485. DOI: [10.1007/s00190-011-0445-3](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00190-011-0445-3).
  * Referenced in Section 02.02 (Chapter 2) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 2.

* **Karney, C. F. F.** (2013). *Algorithms for geodesics.* *Journal of Geodesy*, 87(1), 43-55. DOI: [10.1007/s00190-012-0578-z](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00190-012-0578-z).
  * **Scope:** Exact Ellipsoidal Geodesics, Ellipsoidal Polygon Area, and `GeographicLib`.
  * **Critical Annotation:** Karney reformulated the classical direct and inverse geodesic problems on an oblate ellipsoid of revolution to achieve full double-precision machine accuracy ($<15\text{ nm}$ error anywhere on Earth) and guaranteed rapid convergence (via Newton's method) even for nearly antipodal points where Vincenty's (1975) iterative algorithm fails to converge or loses several digits of precision. Furthermore, Karney derives a rigorous Gauss–Bonnet formulation for the exact surface area of geodesic polygons on the ellipsoid, eliminating the need to project coordinates onto an equal-area plane before computing polygon area.
  * **Key Formulations & Error Budgets:** Expands the geodesic integrals into sixth-order Taylor series in the third flattening $n = (a - b)/(a + b) = f/(2 - f) \approx f/2$, reducing truncation error below $5\text{ nm}$ while bounding ellipsoidal polygon area round-off via Clenshaw summation.
  * **Practitioner Insight:** Implemented directly in `GeographicLib`, `PROJ` (`geod`), PostGIS (`ST_Distance`, `ST_Area` on `geography` types), and Python (`pyproj.Geod`), Karney's algorithms provide the exact metric baseline for evaluating horizontal distance, azimuth, geodesic slope, and variable cell area $A(\phi)$ on geographic (`EPSG:4326`) DEM grids.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 2.

* **Kashani, A., Olsen, M., Parrish, C., & Wilson, N.** (2015). *A Review of LIDAR Radiometric Processing: From Ad Hoc Intensity Correction to Rigorous Radiometric Calibration.* *Sensors*, 15(11), 28099-28128. DOI: [10.3390/s151128099](https://doi.org/10.3390/s151128099).
  * Comprehensive review detailing the physics of laser return amplitude, range-squared loss, incidence angle normalization using DEM normals, and empirical calibration using spectral targets.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 28.

* **Kazhdan, M., & Hoppe, H.** (2013). *Screened poisson surface reconstruction.* *ACM Transactions on Graphics*, 32(3), 1-13. DOI: [10.1145/2487228.2487237](https://doi.org/10.1145/2487228.2487237).
  * *Adds explicit point-position screening constraints to adaptive octree Poisson reconstruction (`PoissonRecon`), preserving sharp edges on overhanging 3D cliffs, arches, and sea caves.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 21.

* **Kerbl, B., Kopanas, G., Leimkuehler, T., & Drettakis, G.** (2023). *3D Gaussian Splatting for Real-Time Radiance Field Rendering.* *ACM Transactions on Graphics*, 42(4), 1-14. DOI: [10.1145/3592433](https://doi.org/10.1145/3592433).
  * Seminal paper introducing 3D Gaussian Splatting, deriving the anisotropic 3D-to-2D covariance projection matrix and GPU tile-based alpha-blending rasterizer.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 25.

* **Kido, M., Osada, Y., Fujimoto, H., Hino, R., & Ito, Y.** (2011). *Trench-normal variation in observed seafloor displacements associated with the 2011 Tohoku-Oki earthquake: SEAFLOOR MOVEMENT OF TOHOKU EARTHQUAKE.* *Geophysical Research Letters*, 38(24), n/a-n/a. DOI: [10.1029/2011GL050057](https://doi.org/10.1029/2011GL050057).
  * Referenced in Section 04.03 (Chapter 4) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 4.

* **Kiessling, F., Nefzger, P., Nolasco, J. F., & Kaintzyk, U.** (2003). *Overhead Power Lines: Planning, Design, Construction.* *Power Systems*. DOI: [10.1007/978-3-642-97879-1](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-97879-1).
  * *Authoritative engineering treatise deriving exact hyperbolic catenary statics, inclined mountain span geometry, wind/ice vector loading, and the cubic thermo-elastic sag-tension state-change equation.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 20.

* **Kim, G., & Kim, A.** (2018). *Scan Context: Egocentric Spatial Descriptor for Place Recognition Within 3D Point Cloud Map.* *2018 IEEE/RSJ International Conference on Intelligent Robots and Systems (IROS)*, 4802-4809. DOI: [10.1109/IROS.2018.8593953](https://doi.org/10.1109/IROS.2018.8593953).
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** Introduces **Scan Context**, a fast, global 3D LiDAR place-recognition descriptor designed to detect loop closures even when revisiting a site from an arbitrary heading angle ($360^\circ$ viewpoint invariance). Partitions an egocentric 3D point cloud $\mathcal{P}$ into $N_r$ radial rings (width $L_{\max}/N_r$) and $N_s$ azimuthal sectors (width $2\pi/N_s$), encoding each bin $(i, j)$ by the maximum point height $z$ within that cell:
    $$I(i, j) = \max_{\mathbf{p} \in \mathcal{P}_{ij}} z(\mathbf{p}), \qquad \mathbf{k}_{\text{ring}}(i) = \frac{\|\mathbf{I}_{i,:}\|_0}{N_s}, \qquad d(\mathbf{I}^q, \mathbf{I}^c) = \min_{n \in [1, N_s]} D_{\text{cos}}\!\bigl(\mathbf{I}^q,\, \operatorname{shift}(\mathbf{I}^c, n)\bigr)$$
    where the rotation-invariant $N_r$-dimensional **ring key** $\mathbf{k}_{\text{ring}}$ enables fast $k$-d tree candidate retrieval, and the column-shift $n^*$ that minimizes the sector-wise cosine distance $D_{\text{cos}}$ directly yields the coarse yaw alignment $\Delta\psi_0 = 2\pi n^*/N_s$ for warm-starting local `ICP` verification.
  * **Validation and Error Budget:** Achieves high precision-recall place recognition in microseconds without requiring dense local surface normals or visual lighting consistency, overcoming the $\sim 15^\circ\text{–}30^\circ$ yaw basin-of-attraction limit of standard `ICP`.
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** Powers automated global loop-closure detection (`SC-LIO-SAM`, `SC-A-LOAM`) when a backpack or mobile mapper crosses an earlier path at an orthogonal or opposite heading.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 6.

* **Kinsey, J. C., Eustice, R. M., & Whitcomb, L. L.** (2006). A survey of underwater vehicle navigation: Recent advances and new challenges. *7th IFAC Conference on Manoeuvring and Control of Marine Craft (MCMC'2006)*, Lisbon, Portugal, 1–12.
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** Comprehensive synthesis of state-of-the-art navigation architectures for deep-submergence **Autonomous Underwater Vehicles (`AUVs`)** and **Remotely Operated Vehicles (`ROVs`)** operating in the GNSS-denied ocean interior. Formulates the error budgets and multisensor fusion of four-beam Janus-configuration **Doppler Velocity Logs (`DVL`)**, north-seeking Fiber-Optic Gyro (`FOG`) / Ring Laser Gyro (`RLG`) strapdown `INS`, ambient Paroscientific quartz pressure-depth gauges, and `LBL`/`USBL` acoustic updates:
    $$\Delta f_{D,i} = \frac{2 f_0}{c_0} v_{\text{beam},i}, \qquad \mathbf{v}_{\text{body}} = \mathbf{J}_{\text{Janus}}^{+} \begin{bmatrix} v_{\text{beam},1} & v_{\text{beam},2} & v_{\text{beam},3} & v_{\text{beam},4} \end{bmatrix}^T, \qquad v_x = \frac{c_0\bigl(\Delta f_{D,1} - \Delta f_{D,2}\bigr)}{4 f_0 \sin\theta_{\text{Janus}}}$$
    showing how differencing opposing Janus beams (tilted at off-nadir angle $\theta_{\text{Janus}} \approx 30^\circ$ from vertical, i.e., depression angle $\beta = 90^\circ - \theta_{\text{Janus}} \approx 60^\circ$ from horizontal, so $\sin\theta_{\text{Janus}} = \cos\beta$) cancels vertical heave velocity error in horizontal speed and—by Snell's law of acoustic refraction ($\frac{\sin\theta(z)}{c(z)} = \frac{\sin\theta_{\text{Janus}}}{c_0}$) in a horizontally stratified water column—makes horizontal bottom-track velocity independent of bulk water-column sound-speed stratification once scaled by transducer face sound speed $c_0$ (and completely independent of $c_0$ for a flat-face phased-array DVL where $\sin\theta_{\text{Janus}} = c_0 / (f_0 \Lambda_{\text{array}})$).
  * **Validation and Error Budget:** Demonstrates that coupling a $300\text{–}1200\text{ kHz}$ bottom-locked `DVL` with a navigation-grade `FOG/RLG INS` bounds submerged dead-reckoning horizontal drift to $0.02\%\text{–}0.1\%$ of distance traveled ($\sim 0.7\text{–}3.6\text{ m hr}^{-1}$ at $1\text{ m s}^{-1}$), compared to kilometers per hour for unaided inertial navigation.
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** Essential for understanding horizontal positioning drift between adjacent AUV multibeam survey lines and how bathymetric SLAM (Chapter 6) or `USBL`/`LBL` fixes constrain deep-ocean DEM mosaicking.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 5.

* **Kirby, J. T., & Dalrymple, R. A.** (1986). *An approximate model for nonlinear dispersion in monochromatic wave propagation models.* *Coastal Engineering*, 9(6), 545-561. DOI: [10.1016/0378-3839(86)90003-7](https://doi.org/10.1016/0378-3839(86)90003-7).

* **Lane, S. N., Westaway, R. M., & Murray Hicks, D.** (2003). *Estimation of erosion and deposition volumes in a large, gravel‐bed, braided river using synoptic remote sensing.* *Earth Surface Processes and Landforms*, 28(3), 249-271. DOI: [10.1002/esp.483](https://doi.org/10.1002/esp.483).
  * **Scope:** DEM of Difference ($\text{DoD}$), Volumetric Error Propagation, and Level of Detection.
  * **Critical Annotation:** This seminal geomorphological paper established the modern statistical framework for DEM of Difference ($\text{DoD} = \hat{z}_{t_2} - \hat{z}_{t_1}$) volumetric sediment budgeting and minimum Level of Detection ($\text{LoD}_{\min} = t\sqrt{\sigma_{z_1}^2 + \sigma_{z_2}^2}$) thresholding. The authors demonstrate how propagating individual elevation uncertainties ($\sigma_{z_1}, \sigma_{z_2}$) into morphological change maps separates real erosion and aggradation from random measurement noise, while highlighting how spatially correlated errors and systematic vertical biases ($\mu_e$) dominate integrated volume errors ($\sigma_V^2$) over large areas.
  * **Practitioner Insight:** The standard methodological reference for conducting multi-temporal earthwork verification, river restoration monitoring, glacial mass balance, or post-dredge volume accounting (implemented in open-source tools such as `xdem` and GCD).
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 1.

DOI: [10.1029/2011JF002140](https://doi.org/10.1029/2011JF002140).

T.** (2013). *The Accidental Tide Gauge: A GPS Reflection Case Study From Kachemak Bay, Alaska.* *IEEE Geoscience and Remote Sensing Letters*, 10(5), 1200-1204. DOI: [10.1109/LGRS.2012.2236075](https://doi.org/10.1109/LGRS.2012.2236075).
  * **Platform & Kinematic Focus:** Repurposes permanently monumented coastal geodetic GNSS Continuously Operating Reference Stations (CORS) as non-contact tide gauges and snow-depth sensors using GNSS Interferometric Reflectometry (GNSS-IR) on low-elevation ($5^\circ\text{–}25^\circ$) multipath Signal-to-Noise Ratio (SNR) fringes.
  * **DEM Uncertainty & Error Budget Implications:** Retrieves antenna-to-water reflector height ($H_R = \frac{\lambda}{2}\frac{d\phi_{\text{SNR}}}{d(\sin e)}$) from the Lomb–Scargle spectral frequency with respect to $\sin e$, formulates the dynamic sea-surface vertical velocity correction ($H_R^{\text{corr}} = H_R^{\text{obs}} - \frac{\dot{H}_R \tan e}{\dot{e}}$), and directly ties local tidal datums (MLLW, LMSL) to the geocentric ITRF ellipsoid while continuously tracking land subsidence.
  * **Validation & Operational Guidance:** Implemented in the open-source Python package `gnssrefl` (`quickLook`, `gnssir`, `subdaily`) with automated tropospheric refraction and tidal-rate corrections.

  * **Mathematical & Geometric Synthesis:** Introduces the **Scale-Invariant Feature Transform (`SIFT`)**, detecting sub-pixel 3D Taylor-interpolated extrema in a Difference-of-Gaussian (DoG) scale-space pyramid, assigning canonical dominant orientations, and encoding local gradient histograms into a 128-dimensional vector paired with the second-nearest-neighbor distance ratio test ($d_1 / d_2 < 0.8$).
  * **DEM Generation & Error Significance:** Enables automated tie-point extraction across uncalibrated UAV, handheld, and AUV image blocks with large scale ($>2\times$) and viewing-angle ($>30^\circ$) variations, directly determining the spatial density and sub-pixel measurement noise ($\sigma_{u,v} \approx 0.1\text{–}0.3\text{ px}$) of sparse bundle adjustment control networks.
  * **Operational & Software Implementation:** Implemented across `SIFTGPU`, `VLFeat`, `OpenCV`, `COLMAP`, `MicMac` (`Tapioca`), and `ASP` (`ipfind`) as the default tie-point detector.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 8.

  * **Platform & Kinematic Focus:** Benchmarks consumer solid-state Vertical-Cavity Surface-Emitting Laser (VCSEL) direct Time-of-Flight (dToF) LiDAR integrated with visual-inertial odometry (VIO) on handheld smartphones against research-grade Terrestrial Laser Scanning (TLS) and SfM photogrammetry.
  * **DEM Uncertainty & Error Budget Implications:** Quantifies the $5\text{ m}$ effective range limit, spatial resolution smoothing on centimeter-scale micro-topography, and VIO trajectory drift over tens of meters on coastal cliffs, establishing accuracy bounds ($\pm 1\text{–}10\text{ cm}$ local) for crowd-sourced pedestrian elevation capture.

  * **Scope:** ISBN: `978-3-540-78480-7` | Sonar Physics, Beamforming, and Acoustic Ray Tracing.
  * **Critical Annotation:** Lurton provides the authoritative physical foundation for how acoustic waves propagate through an inhomogeneous, stratified ocean and scatter off the seafloor. The book derives the active sonar equations, frequency-dependent acoustic absorption, Snell's law ray-tracing refraction through vertical sound speed profiles $c(z)$, beamforming geometry, and phase- versus amplitude-bottom detection in multibeam echosounders.
  * **Practitioner Insight:** Essential reading for diagnosing why outer-beam refraction errors ("smiles" and "frowns"), roll/pitch lever-arm misalignments, or low-frequency soft-sediment acoustic penetration corrupt bathymetric DEMs and their derived slope fields.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 1, Chapter 7, Chapter 10, Chapter 28.

  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 28.

  * *Core Radiative Transfer Contribution:* Derives the multi-spectral radiative transfer equations linearizing two-way water-column absorption via deep-water radiance subtraction ($X_i = \ln[R_i - R_{\infty,i}]$), establishing multi-band linear regression for shallow bathymetry.
  * *Engineering & DEM Relevance:* The classical benchmark model for coastal satellite bathymetry from Landsat, SPOT, and airborne multispectral scanners.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 9, Chapter 11.

* **Mackenzie, K. V.** (1981). *Nine-term equation for sound speed in the oceans.* *The Journal of the Acoustical Society of America*, 70(3), 807-812. DOI: [10.1121/1.386920](https://doi.org/10.1121/1.386920).
  * *Core Mathematical Contribution:* Derives a compact 9-term empirical polynomial for sound speed $c(T, S, z)$ across $T \in [2, 30]^\circ\text{C}$, $S \in [25, 40]\text{ PSU}$, and depth $z \in [0, 8000]\text{ m}$ with a standard error of $0.070\text{ m/s}$.
  * *Engineering & DEM Relevance:* Directly computes sound speed as a function of geometric depth $z$ (meters) rather than hydrostatic gauge pressure $P$, making it the standard lightweight equation embedded in real-time sonar transceivers and educational ray-tracing engines.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 10.

  * Referenced in Section 07.01 (Chapter 7) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.

* **Maiman, T. H.** (1960). *Stimulated Optical Radiation in Ruby.* *Nature*, 187(4736), 493-494. DOI: [10.1038/187493a0](https://doi.org/10.1038/187493a0).
  * *Core Physical Contribution:* Documents the first experimental realization of optical amplification via stimulated emission in a synthetic pink ruby ($\text{Cr}^{3+}:\text{Al}_2\text{O}_3$) crystal pumped by a pulsed xenon flashlamp at $\lambda = 694.3\text{ nm}$. By achieving population inversion between metastable energy states and demonstrating monochromatic spatial and temporal coherence, Maiman established the physical basis for active optical time-of-flight (ToF) ranging.
  * *Engineering & DEM Relevance:* Every modern airborne, terrestrial, and spaceborne laser altimeter—from $1064\text{ nm}$ $\text{Nd:YAG}$ and $\text{Yb}$-doped fiber lasers to frequency-doubled $532\text{ nm}$ hydrographic transceivers—relies on the narrow spectral linewidth (enabling narrowband solar background rejection filters) and nanosecond-to-picosecond $Q$-switched or mode-locked pulse generation descended from this solid-state resonator architecture.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 9.

* **Mallet, C., & Bretar, F.** (2009). *Full-waveform topographic lidar: State-of-the-art.* *ISPRS Journal of Photogrammetry and Remote Sensing*, 64(1), 1-16. DOI: [10.1016/j.isprsjprs.2008.09.007](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.isprsjprs.2008.09.007).
  * *Core Mathematical Contribution:* Compares parametric waveform decomposition methods (generalized Gaussian, log-normal, and Weibull mixture models) against non-parametric deconvolution algorithms (Wiener filtering, Richardson–Lucy, and B-spline fitting) for small- and large-footprint systems.
  * *Engineering & DEM Relevance:* Establishes how echo pulse broadening ($\hat{s}_i > s_0$) directly encodes within-footprint vertical roughness and local terrain slope, providing a decisive radiometric-geometric feature for rejecting false ground returns in marsh grass, salt-marsh cordgrass (*Spartina*), scree, and forest litter.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 9.

* **Markus, T., Neumann, T., Martino, A., Abdalati, W., Brunt, K., Csatho, B., Farrell, S., Fricker, H., et al.** (2017). *The Ice, Cloud, and land Elevation Satellite-2 (ICESat-2): Science requirements, concept, and implementation.* *Remote Sensing of Environment*, 190, 260-273. DOI: [10.1016/j.rse.2016.12.029](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.rse.2016.12.029).
  * **Platform & Kinematic Focus:** Details the spaceborne photon-counting laser altimeter architecture of ICESat-2 (ATLAS, $532\text{ nm}$ green laser, $10\text{ kHz}$ pulse rate, six beams arranged in three pairs with $90\text{ m}$ intra-pair spacing and $3.3\text{ km}$ inter-pair spacing).
  * **DEM Uncertainty & Error Budget Implications:** Explains how the dual-beam pair geometry separates cross-track surface slope from true elevation change on a single orbital pass, while Precision Orbit Determination (POD) and Precision Attitude Determination (PAD) achieve $< 10\text{ cm}$ vertical accuracy globally—providing the primary spaceborne vertical control backbone for both terrestrial DEM co-registration and nearshore bathymetric calibration.
  * **Validation & Operational Guidance:** Defines the geolocation (`ATL03`) and land/canopy (`ATL08`) photon processing frameworks used in `SlideRule` and `xdem` to remove orbital ramps and vertical datum biases from optical and radar DEMs.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 7, Chapter 9, Chapter 14, Chapter 24.

* **Mastrogiuseppe, M., Poggiali, V., Hayes, A., Lorenz, R., Lunine, J., Picardi, G., Seu, R., Flamini, E., et al.** (2014). *The bathymetry of a Titan sea.* *Geophysical Research Letters*, 41(5), 1432-1437. DOI: [10.1002/2013GL058618](https://doi.org/10.1002/2013GL058618).
  * Landmark discovery documenting Cassini RADAR altimetry penetration through liquid methane/ethane seas on Titan, measuring seabed bathymetry down to $160\text{ m}$ depth in Ligeia Mare.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 31.

* **Matheron, G.** (1973). *The intrinsic random functions and their applications.* *Advances in Applied Probability*, 5(3), 439-468. DOI: [10.2307/1425829](https://doi.org/10.2307/1425829).
  * Mathematical masterwork deriving intrinsic random functions of order $k$ and the Turning Bands Method for multi-dimensional spatial stochastic simulation.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 33.

DOI: [10.1016/j.isprsjprs.2016.04.011](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.isprsjprs.2016.04.011).
  * *Comprehensive synthesis of airborne/UAS LiDAR, synthetic aperture radar, and stereo photogrammetry capabilities and failure modes for conductor catenary extraction and corridor vegetation auditing.*

* **Maune, D. F., & Nayegandhi, A. (Eds.).** (2018). *Digital Elevation Model Technologies and Applications: The DEM Users Manual* (3rd ed.; 1st ed., 2001 & 2nd ed., 2007 edited by D. F. Maune). Bethesda, MD: American Society for Photogrammetry and Remote Sensing (ASPRS).
  * **Scope:** ISBN: `978-1-57083-102-7` (3rd ed. print), `978-1-57083-103-4` (3rd ed. eBook), `978-1-57083-085-3` (2nd ed., 2007) | Terrestrial & Topobathymetric DEM Production.
  * **Critical Annotation:** Widely regarded as the definitive industry and government reference for operational elevation data production, this multi-author volume bridges the gap between sensor physics and project-level engineering specifications. It provides exhaustive coverage of airborne topographic lidar, Geiger-mode and single-photon lidar, IfSAR/InSAR, photogrammetric Structure-from-Motion (SfM), and topobathymetric green-wavelength ($532\text{ nm}$) laser systems. Furthermore, it systematically contrasts bare-earth Digital Terrain Models (DTMs), first-return Digital Surface Models (DSMs), and Triangulated Irregular Networks (TINs) across civil, hydrologic, and aviation domains.
  * **Practitioner Insight:** Essential for its user requirements matrices, quality assessment workflows, hydro-flattening versus hydro-enforcement semantics, and practical guidance on implementing the ASPRS Positional Accuracy Standards across national mapping programs such as the USGS 3D Elevation Program (3DEP).
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 1.

* **Maurer, J. M., Schaefer, J. M., Rupper, S., & Corley, A.** (2019). *Acceleration of ice loss across the Himalayas over the past 40 years.* *Science Advances*, 5(6), eaav7266. DOI: [10.1126/sciadv.aav7266](https://doi.org/10.1126/sciadv.aav7266).
  * **Platform & Kinematic Focus:** Operationalizes declassified Cold War reconnaissance film from the KH-9 HEXAGON mapping camera (1973–1980) alongside modern ASTER and WorldView optical stereo satellites across a $2000\text{ km}$ Himalayan arc.
  * **DEM Uncertainty & Error Budget Implications:** Details $7\text{ }\mu\text{m}$ photogrammetric film scanning, reseau-grid film shrinkage/warping correction, 3D co-registration against stable off-ice terrain, and spatially autocorrelated uncertainty propagation for multidecadal elevation change ($\Delta z / \Delta t$) budgets.
  * **Validation & Operational Guidance:** Serves as the benchmark workflow for historical spy-satellite DEM extraction implemented in `ASP` and `kh9_preprocessing` pipelines.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 7.

* **Mayer, L. A.** (2006). *Frontiers in Seafloor Mapping and Visualization.* *Marine Geophysical Researches*, 27(1), 7-17. DOI: [10.1007/s11001-005-0267-x](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11001-005-0267-x).
  * *Core Methodological Contribution:* Synthesizes the paradigm shift from sparse single-beam profiling to high-density multibeam swath mapping, quantitative acoustic backscatter characterization, uncertainty-weighted surface estimation, and interactive 3D/4D scientific visualization.
  * *Engineering & DEM Relevance:* Demonstrates how integrating beam footprint geometry, Total Propagated Uncertainty (TPU), and water-column imaging transforms raw acoustic point clouds into quantitative benthic habitat and geomorphological DEMs.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 10.

* **Mayer, L., Jakobsson, M., Allen, G., Dorschel, B., Falconer, R., Ferrini, V., Lamarche, G., Snaith, H., et al.** (2018). *The Nippon Foundation—GEBCO Seabed 2030 Project: The Quest to See the World’s Oceans Completely Mapped by 2030.* *Geosciences*, 8(2), 63. DOI: [10.3390/geosciences8020063](https://doi.org/10.3390/geosciences8020063).
  * **Scope:** Global Ocean Mapping, Multi-Resolution Compilation, and Provenance Metadata.
  * **Critical Annotation:** This paper lays out the scientific rationale, depth-dependent resolution targets (from $100\text{ m}$ in depths $<1,500\text{ m}$ to $800\text{ m}$ in hadal trenches), and technical architecture for the global Seabed 2030 initiative. The authors quantify the stark operational reality that over 75% of the global ocean floor remains unmapped by direct acoustic soundings, leaving critical blind spots for tsunami modeling, deep-ocean circulation sills, and submarine navigation.
  * **Practitioner Insight:** Establishes the modern framework for multi-resolution bathymetric compilation ("remove-restore" blending) and explicit cell-by-cell provenance tracking via Type Identifier (TID) grids in global and regional ocean DEMs.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 1, Chapter 23, Chapter 24.

II: Measurement, Modeling, and Management.* *Journal of Hydraulic Engineering*, 133(1), 23-38. DOI: [10.1061/(ASCE)0733-9429(2007)133:1(23)](https://doi.org/10.1061/(ASCE)0733-9429(2007)133:1(23)).

  * Exhaustive historical and technical reference on modern timekeeping: sidereal time, UT1, TAI, UTC, leap-second insertions, and atomic cesium resonance standards.

* **McHarg, I. L.** (1969). *Design with Nature*. Garden City, NY: Natural History Press (Doubleday).
  * **Scope:** ISBN: `978-0-471-11460-4` *(25th Anniversary Edition, John Wiley & Sons, 1995)* | Multi-Criteria Overlay Analysis & Environmental Planning.
  * **Critical Annotation:** One of the most influential works in the history of environmental planning and Geographic Information Systems, *Design with Nature* formalized the multi-layer suitability overlay methodology that underpins modern raster and vector GIS analysis. By stacking transparent Mylars encoded with topographic slope $\|\nabla z\|$, surface drainage, tidal and riverine floodplain inundation risk, bedrock geology, and ecological value, McHarg demonstrated how physical landscape processes must constrain civil infrastructure and urban development.
  * **Practitioner Insight:** Every modern multi-criteria DEM workflow—from highway corridor optimization and renewable energy siting to coastal resilience planning and C. Dana Tomlin's Map Algebra—is a direct mathematical descendant of McHarg's composite cost-surface paradigm.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 1.

  * Establishes the IERS-standard closed-form hydrostatic and non-hydrostatic zenith delay equations for optical satellite laser ranging and spaceborne laser altimetry ($532\text{ nm}$ and $1064\text{ nm}$).

  * Seminal paper establishing the Network Time Protocol (NTP), detailing packet filter algorithms, clock discipline loops, and round-trip delay estimation.

* **Milne, P. H.** (1983). *Underwater Acoustic Positioning Systems*. Gulf Publishing / E. & F. N. Spon. ISBN: 978-0-87201-012-3.
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** The classic engineering monograph establishing the geometric and acoustic principles of **Long Baseline (`LBL`)**, **Short Baseline (`SBL`)**, and **Ultra-Short Baseline (`USBL` / `SSBL`)** subsea positioning. Formulates spherical/hyperbolic range trilateration for seafloor transponder arrays (`LBL`) and phase-comparison direction-of-arrival (`DOA`) bearing estimation for hull-mounted multi-element transducer arrays (`USBL`), where electrical phase difference $\Delta\psi_{\text{elec}}$ across baseline element spacing $d_{\text{elem}}$ yields mechanical incidence angle $\theta_{\text{inc}}$:
    $$\Delta\psi_{\text{elec}} = \frac{2\pi f_{\text{ac}} d_{\text{elem}}}{c_0}\sin\theta_{\text{inc}}, \qquad \mathbf{r}_{\text{USBL}}^b = R_{\text{slant}} \begin{bmatrix} \sin\theta_x \\ \sin\theta_y \\ \sqrt{1 - \sin^2\theta_x - \sin^2\theta_y} \end{bmatrix}$$
    where $f_{\text{ac}}$ is the acoustic carrier frequency (in $\text{Hz}$), $c_0$ is the face sound velocity at the transducer head (in $\text{m s}^{-1}$), and $R_{\text{slant}} = \frac{1}{2}\bar{c}_{\text{eff}}\,\Delta t_{\text{twtt}}$ is the ray-traced slant range (in $\text{m}$).
  * **Validation and Error Budget:** Propagates acoustic phase-angle variance $\sigma_{\theta,\text{DOA}}^2$, vessel `INS` attitude variance $\sigma_{\phi,\text{IMU}}^2$, and transducer alignment bias variance $\sigma_{\text{align}}^2$ into horizontal subsea target uncertainty:
    $$\sigma_{XY,\text{USBL}}^2 \approx R_{\text{slant}}^2\bigl(\sigma_{\theta,\text{DOA}}^2 + \sigma_{\phi,\text{IMU}}^2 + \sigma_{\text{align}}^2\bigr) + \sigma_{R,\text{slant}}^2\sin^2\theta_{\text{inc}}$$
    demonstrating that a calibrated `USBL` ($0.15^\circ\text{–}0.30^\circ$ $1\sigma$ angular accuracy, or $0.25\%\text{–}0.5\%$ of slant range) incurs $\pm 7.5\text{–}15.0\text{ m}$ horizontal uncertainty at $R_{\text{slant}} = 3{,}000\text{ m}$, whereas a seafloor-moored `LBL` array achieves depth-independent $\sigma_{XY,\text{LBL}} = \text{HDOP}_{\text{LBL}}\,\sigma_R \approx 0.02\text{–}0.15\text{ m}$.
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** Explains why `USBL` horizontal position uncertainty scales linearly with depth, necessitating seafloor `LBL` arrays, inverted `USBL`, or `DVL`-aided `INS` for centimeter-scale deep-water AUV/ROV bathymetric mapping.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 5.

* **Misra, P., & Enge, P.** (2011). *Global Positioning System: Signals, Measurements, and Performance* (Rev. 2nd ed.; orig. 2nd ed. 2006). Lincoln, MA: Ganga-Jamuna Press. ISBN: 978-0-9709544-1-1.
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** The definitive graduate textbook on satellite radio-navigation physics, signal structure, pseudorange and carrier-phase observation equations, and position-domain dilution of precision (DOP). Formally derives the $m \times 4$ linearized geometry matrix $\mathbf{G}$ whose $s$-th row $\mathbf{G}_{s,:} = \bigl[-(\mathbf{e}_r^s)^T,\; 1\bigr] = \bigl[-\cos E^s \sin A^s,\; -\cos E^s \cos A^s,\; -\sin E^s,\; 1\bigr]$ relates the receiver-to-satellite line-of-sight unit vector $\mathbf{e}_r^s = [\cos E^s \sin A^s,\; \cos E^s \cos A^s,\; \sin E^s]^T$ (at satellite elevation angle $E^s \ge 0$ and azimuth $A^s$ clockwise from North) and receiver clock offset $c\,\delta t_r$ (in $\text{m}$) to the $4 \times 4$ local East–North–Up and clock cofactor matrix $\mathbf{Q} = (\mathbf{G}^T\mathbf{G})^{-1}$:
    $$\text{HDOP} = \sqrt{Q_{EE} + Q_{NN}}, \qquad \text{VDOP} = \sqrt{Q_{UU}}, \qquad \sigma_h = \sigma_{\text{UERE}}\,\text{VDOP}$$
    where $\sigma_{\text{UERE}}$ is the user equivalent range error (in $\text{m}$) and $\sigma_h$ is the vertical ellipsoidal height standard deviation (in $\text{m}$).
  * **Validation and Error Budget:** Proves analytically why one-sided sky visibility ($E^s \in [5^\circ, 90^\circ]$, so $-\sin E^s < 0$ for every satellite while the clock column is $+1$) forces strong collinearity between the upward vertical coordinate $\delta U$, the receiver clock bias $c\,\delta t_r$, and the zenith tropospheric delay $T_z$, causing $\text{VDOP}$ to exceed $\text{HDOP}$ by a factor of $1.5\times$ to $3.0\times$ across all terrestrial and marine kinematic platforms.
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** Essential reading for understanding why vertical ellipsoidal height $h$ is the weakest component of any GNSS trajectory and why elevation masks, constellation geometry, and multi-frequency observables directly govern vertical DEM accuracy ($\text{TVU}$).
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 5.

* **Mitasova, H., Mitas, L., & Harmon, R. S.** (2005). *Simultaneous Spline Approximation and Topographic Analysis for Lidar Elevation Data in Open-Source GIS.* *IEEE Geoscience and Remote Sensing Letters*, 2(4), 375-379. DOI: [10.1109/LGRS.2005.848533](https://doi.org/10.1109/LGRS.2005.848533).

* **Moritz, H.** (1980). *Geodetic reference system 1980.* *Bulletin Géodésique*, 54(3), 395-405. DOI: [10.1007/BF02521480](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF02521480).
  * **Scope:** (1980); (2000) | IUGG/IAG Official Specification of the GRS80 Reference Ellipsoid.
  * **Critical Annotation:** Adopted at the XVII General Assembly of the International Union of Geodesy and Geophysics (IUGG) in Canberra in December 1979, this specification defines the Geodetic Reference System 1980 (GRS80) via four exact physical and geometric defining constants: the equatorial semi-major axis $a = 6,378,137\text{ m}$, the geocentric gravitational constant $GM = 3,986,005 \times 10^8\text{ m}^3\text{ s}^{-2}$, the dynamical form factor $J_2 = 108,263 \times 10^{-8}$, and the nominal mean angular velocity of the Earth $\omega = 7,292,115 \times 10^{-11}\text{ rad s}^{-1}$.
  * **Key Formulations & Error Budgets:** From the four exact defining parameters, Moritz solves the closed-form equipotential condition to derive all secondary geometric constants, including first eccentricity squared $e^2 = 0.006\,694\,380\,022\,90$, reciprocal flattening $1/f = 298.257\,222\,101$, and polar semi-minor axis $b = 6,356,752.314140\text{ m}$.
  * **Practitioner Insight:** Explains the exact origin of the $0.105\text{ mm}$ discrepancy in polar semi-minor axis $b$ between GRS80 ($b = 6,356,752.314140\text{ m}$, used by ITRF conversions, NAD83, NATRF2022, and ETRS89) and WGS84 ($1/f = 298.257\,223\,563 \implies b = 6,356,752.314245\text{ m}$, which resulted from rounding the normalized second-degree zonal harmonic $\bar{C}_{2,0}$ to eight significant digits in the 1984 Defense Mapping Agency derivation). This proves why GRS80 and WGS84 *ellipsoids* are geometrically identical at sub-millimeter precision while their *reference frames* differ by $1.2\text{--}2.2\text{ m}$.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 2.

* **Munk, W. H.** (1966). *Abyssal recipes.* *Deep Sea Research and Oceanographic Abstracts*, 13(4), 707-730. DOI: [10.1016/0011-7471(66)90602-4](https://doi.org/10.1016/0011-7471(66)90602-4).

### N

* **National Geodetic Survey (NGS).** (2021). *Blueprint for the Modernized NSRS, Part 2: Geopotential Coordinates and Geopotential Datum*. NOAA Technical Report NOS NGS 64 (Updated ed.). Silver Spring, MD: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration. 72 pp.
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** Defines the architectural replacement of NAVD88 and U.S. island vertical datums (PRVD02, VIVD09, ASVD02, GUVD04, NMVD03) with the **North American-Pacific Geopotential Datum of 2022 (NAPGD2022)**. Anchored to the adopted reference equipotential constant $W_0 = 62{,}636{,}856.0\text{ m}^2\text{ s}^{-2}$, NAPGD2022 realizes orthometric heights entirely through GNSS positioning (`ITRF2020` / `NATRF2022`) and the high-resolution gravimetric geoid `GEOID2022` (`SGEOID2022`), augmented by a dynamic time-dependent velocity grid (`DGEOID2022`) tracking Glacial Isostatic Adjustment (GIA) and secular mass redistribution:
    $$H(t) = h_{\text{ITRF2020/NATRF2022}}(t) - \left[N_{\text{GEOID2022}}(t_0) + \dot{N}_{\text{DGEOID2022}} \cdot (t - t_0)\right]$$
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** Essential reading for planning the multi-decadal migration of national elevation archives (such as USGS 3DEP and NOAA CUDEM), where adopting NAPGD2022 introduces a spatially smoothly varying orthometric height shift ranging from $-0.3\text{ m}$ in southern Florida to $-1.3\text{ m}$ in the Pacific Northwest relative to NAVD88.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 3.

* **National Geospatial-Intelligence Agency (NGA).** (2014/2021/2024). *Department of Defense World Geodetic System 1984: Its Definition and Relationships with Local Geodetic Systems* (NGA Standardization Document `NGA.STND.0036_1.0.0_WGS84`, 2014; Addendum on WGS 84 (G2139), 2021; Update to WGS 84 (G2296), 2024). Springfield, VA: NGA.
  * **Scope:** Technical Standard `NGA.STND.0036` | Official Specification of WGS84 Realizations (`TRANSIT` through `G730`–`G2296`).
  * **Critical Annotation:** Chronicles the evolution of the World Geodetic System 1984 from its initial 1984 Doppler (TRANSIT) realization ($\pm 1\text{--}2\text{ m}$ accuracy) through seven successive GPS/GNSS-based realizations (`G730`, `G873`, `G1150`, `G1674`, `G1762`, `G2139`, and `G2296`) aligned at the centimeter level with contemporary ITRF realizations (`ITRF91` through `ITRF2020`).
  * **Key Formulations & Error Budgets:** Documents the four defining parameters ($a = 6,378,137\text{ m}$, $1/f = 298.257\,223\,563$, $GM = 3.986\,004\,418 \times 10^{14}\text{ m}^3\text{ s}^{-2}$, $\omega = 7.292\,115 \times 10^{-5}\text{ rad s}^{-1}$), the Earth Gravitational Model (EGM96/EGM2008) coupling, and legacy Molodensky datum shifts.
  * **Practitioner Insight:** Explains why generic `EPSG:4326` ("WGS 84" datum ensemble) carries an official positional uncertainty of up to $2.0\text{ m}$ in the EPSG registry and why high-accuracy topobathymetric DEMs certified under ASPRS or IHO S-44/S-102 standards must explicitly record the specific dynamic realization and coordinate epoch (e.g., `EPSG:9754`/`9755` for WGS 84 (G2139) 3D/2D or `EPSG:10605`/`10606` for WGS 84 (G2296) 3D/2D).
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 2.

* **National Institute of Standards and Technology (NIST) & National Geodetic Survey (NGS).** (2020). Deprecation of the United States (U.S.) Survey Foot. *Federal Register*, 85(193), 62698–62708 (October 5, 2020; Docket No. `200921-0249`).
  * **Scope:** *Federal Register* Doc. `2020-21902` | Official Federal Retirement of the U.S. Survey Foot (`EPSG:9003`) in Favor of the International Foot (`EPSG:9002`).
  * **Critical Annotation:** Documents the 1893 Mendenhall Order definition of the U.S. Survey Foot ($1\text{ ft}_{\text{US}} = 1200/3937\text{ m} \approx 0.304\,800\,609\,601\,219\text{ m}$), the 1959 international yard and pound agreement that left the U.S. Survey Foot as a temporary exception for geodetic surveying, and the formal joint NIST/NOAA decision to deprecate the U.S. Survey Foot on December 31, 2022, standardizing exclusively on the International Foot ($1\text{ ft}_{\text{int}} = 0.3048\text{ m}$ exactly).
  * **Key Formulations & Error Budgets:** Quantifies how the $2.000\,004\text{ ppm}$ ratio ($1\text{ ft}_{\text{US}} / 1\text{ ft}_{\text{int}} = 1/0.999998$) produces a systematic horizontal coordinate shift of $\Delta E = 2 \times 10^{-6} \cdot E$—reaching $1.22\text{ m}$ at a false easting of $2,000,000\text{ ft}$ and $>6\text{ m}$ at $10,000,000\text{ ft}$.
  * **Practitioner Insight:** Critical for preventing multi-meter horizontal misalignments when ingesting state-plane lidar LAS/LAZ tiles, municipal stormwater/culvert databases, or USACE/NOAA hydrographic channel design templates across U.S. jurisdictions.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 2.

* **National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration [NOAA HSSD]** (2024). *NOS Hydrographic Surveys Specifications and Deliverables (HSSD)*. Silver Spring, MD: NOAA Office of Coast Survey.

  * **Critical Annotation:** Updated annually by NOAA's Office of Coast Survey, the **HSSD** translates international IHO S-44 principles into rigorous, contract-binding deliverable specifications for U.S. nautical charting surveys, while the companion **Field Procedures Manual (FPM, 1997/2020)** codifies the operational field workflows executed aboard hydrographic vessels and launches. Together, they mandate exact procedures for GNSS/INS trajectory accuracy, sound speed cast frequency, multibeam calibration patch tests (roll, pitch, yaw, and latency), variable-resolution (VR) and fixed-resolution CUBE grid node densities, Danger to Navigation (DTON) reporting, and designated-sounding overrides that force the gridded surface to honor the shoalest acoustic return over any hazard.

* **Nuth, C., & Kääb, A.** (2011). *Co-registration and bias corrections of satellite elevation data sets for quantifying glacier thickness change.* *The Cryosphere*, 5(1), 271-290. DOI: [10.5194/tc-5-271-2011](https://doi.org/10.5194/tc-5-271-2011).
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** Derives the analytical three-dimensional DEM co-registration equation relating apparent vertical elevation differences $\Delta z = z_2(x,y) - z_1(x,y)$ (in $\text{m}$, positive-upward) on stable control terrain of slope angle $\alpha$ and downslope aspect $\psi$ (clockwise from North) to an unknown 3D translation vector $(\Delta E, \Delta N, \Delta z_0) = (a\sin b,\, a\cos b,\, c\,\overline{\tan\alpha})$ of horizontal magnitude $a = \sqrt{\Delta E^2 + \Delta N^2}$ (in $\text{m}$) and shift azimuth $b = \operatorname{atan2}(\Delta E, \Delta N)$:
    $$\frac{\Delta z}{\tan\alpha} = a\cos(b - \psi) + c, \qquad c = \frac{\Delta z_0}{\overline{\tan\alpha}}$$
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** The algorithmic core of `xdem.coreg.NuthKaab`, allowing practitioners to diagnose and remove unmodeled secular tectonic translations or sensor geolocation shifts from multi-temporal terrestrial and bathymetric DEM pairs before computing vertical elevation changes, Normalized Median Absolute Deviation ($\text{NMAD} = 1.4826 \times \operatorname{median}(|\Delta z - \tilde{\Delta z}|)$), and $95\%$ Linear Error ($\text{LE95} = 1.96\,\text{RMSE}_z$).
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 4, Chapter 7, Chapter 14, Chapter 22, Chapter 30.

### O

* **Okada, Y.** (1985). *Surface deformation due to shear and tensile faults in a half-space.* *Bulletin of the Seismological Society of America*, 75(4), 1135-1154. DOI: [10.1785/BSSA0750041135](https://doi.org/10.1785/BSSA0750041135).
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** Derives complete, closed-form analytical algebraic expressions (free of numerical singularities at vertical fault dips $\delta = 90^\circ$ or along fault edges) for the 3D surface displacements $(u_x, u_y, u_z)$ (in $\text{m}$), tilts $(\partial u_z/\partial x, \partial u_z/\partial y)$, and strains $(\partial u_i/\partial x_j)$ produced by a finite rectangular fault plane of along-strike length $L$, down-dip width $W$, burial depth $d$, and dip $\delta$ undergoing uniform strike-slip ($U_1$), dip-slip ($U_2$), or tensile opening ($U_3$) within a homogeneous, isotropic elastic half-space (parameterized by Lamé constants $\lambda, \mu$ and Poisson's ratio $\nu = \frac{\lambda}{2(\lambda+\mu)}$). Built via Chinnery's (1961) substitution notation $\bigl[f(\xi,\eta)\bigr]\parallel$ over Volterra's elastostatic Somigliana dislocation integral across fault surface $\Sigma$ with unit fault normal $n_k(\boldsymbol{\xi})$ and Mindlin point-force Green's function $u_i^j(\mathbf{x}_0;\boldsymbol{\xi})$ for a point body force of magnitude $F$:
    $$u_i(\mathbf{x}_0) = \frac{1}{F}\iint_{\Sigma} \Delta u_j(\boldsymbol{\xi})\left[\lambda\delta_{jk}\frac{\partial u_i^n(\mathbf{x}_0;\boldsymbol{\xi})}{\partial \xi_n} + \mu\left(\frac{\partial u_i^j(\mathbf{x}_0;\boldsymbol{\xi})}{\partial \xi_k} + \frac{\partial u_i^k(\mathbf{x}_0;\boldsymbol{\xi})}{\partial \xi_j}\right)\right]n_k(\boldsymbol{\xi})\,d\Sigma(\boldsymbol{\xi})$$
  * **Validation and Error Budget:** Eliminates the algebraic sign errors and singular branch-cut discontinuities present in earlier Mansinha & Smylie (1971) and Sato & Matsu'ura (1974) formulations, providing an exact analytical benchmark against which finite-element crustal models and InSAR/GNSS slip inversions are verified.
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** The universal physical forward model used to invert GNSS and InSAR coseismic offsets for finite-fault slip distributions, to synthesize tsunami seafloor vertical uplift/subsidence initial conditions ($\Delta u_z$), and to generate continuous coseismic deformation grids when empirical survey coverage contains spatial gaps.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 4.

* **Olson, E.** (2011). *AprilTag: A robust and flexible visual fiducial system.* *2011 IEEE International Conference on Robotics and Automation*, 3400-3407. DOI: [10.1109/ICRA.2011.5979561](https://doi.org/10.1109/ICRA.2011.5979561).
  * *The foundational paper detailing the 2D visual barcode encoding, edge detection, and homography decoding algorithms utilized in modern automated photogrammetric targets.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 12.

* **Open Geospatial Consortium [OGC COG 1.0]** (2023). *OGC Cloud Optimized GeoTIFF Standard, Version 1.0* (OGC Document 21-026r2). Wayland, MA: Open Geospatial Consortium.
  * *The formal international standard codifying the internal tiling, overview pyramid structure, and HTTP Range header layout for Cloud-Optimized GeoTIFFs (COGs).*

* **Open Geospatial Consortium [OGC GeoTIFF 1.1]** (2019). *OGC GeoTIFF Standard, Version 1.1* (OGC Document 19-008r4). Wayland, MA: Open Geospatial Consortium.

* **Parker, B. B.** (2007). *Tidal Analysis and Prediction*. NOAA Special Publication NOS CO-OPS 3. Silver Spring, MD: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration, Center for Operational Oceanographic Products and Services. 378 pp.
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** The comprehensive operational treatise on harmonic tidal analysis, astronomical tide-producing forces, shallow-water nonlinear overtides and compound tides ($M_4, M_6, MS_4, MN_4$), the Rayleigh criterion for constituent frequency separation ($\Delta f \cdot T_{\text{record}} \ge 1$), the 18.61-year regression of the lunar nodes, and the 19-year National Tidal Datum Epoch (NTDE).
  * **Key Formulation and Chapter Pointers:** Chapters 3 and 7 detail the nonlinear momentum advection ($\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u}$), quadratic bottom friction ($C_d \mathbf{u}|\mathbf{u}|/D$), and continuity effects that distort symmetric offshore tidal waves into asymmetric flood-/ebb-dominant estuarine waves, altering the vertical spacing between MHHW, MHW, LMSL, MLW, and MLLW.
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** Explains the Method of Simultaneous Comparisons used to transfer tidal datums from 19-year primary control stations to short-term ($1\text{–}12\text{ month}$) subordinate gauges, and clarifies why linear spatial interpolation of tidal datums fails across barrier inlets and tidal rivers.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 3.

* **Parker, R. L.** (1973). *The Rapid Calculation of Potential Anomalies.* *Geophysical Journal International*, 31(4), 447-455. DOI: [10.1111/j.1365-246X.1973.tb06513.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1365-246X.1973.tb06513.x).
  * *Core Mathematical Contribution:* Formulates the exact 2D Fourier series expansion relating interface topography powers $b^n(\mathbf{x})$ to upward-continued potential-field (gravity and magnetic) anomalies via $\exp(-|\mathbf{k}| d_0)$.
  * *Engineering & DEM Relevance:* Provides the forward and inverse spectral operator (paired with Oldenburg, 1974) underlying `GMT grdfft` and global gravimetric bathymetry prediction.

* **Parrish, C., Magruder, L., Neuenschwander, A., Forfinski-Sarkozi, N., Alonzo, M., & Jasinski, M.** (2019). *Validation of ICESat-2 ATLAS Bathymetry and Analysis of ATLAS’s Bathymetric Mapping Performance.* *Remote Sensing*, 11(14), 1634. DOI: [10.3390/rs11141634](https://doi.org/10.3390/rs11141634).
  * *Core Mathematical Contribution:* Demonstrates that the spaceborne green ($532\text{ nm}$) photon-counting laser altimeter (`ATLAS`) aboard NASA's *ICESat-2* satellite maps nearshore bathymetry to depths approaching $40\text{ m}$ ($1.5\text{–}2.0\text{ Secchi depths}$) in clear oligotrophic waters. Derives the exact trigonometric planimetric ($\Delta Y_{\text{corr}}$) and vertical ($\Delta Z_{\text{corr}}$) refraction corrections required to transform raw `ATL03` georeferenced photon heights—which assume atmospheric light speed $c_{\text{air}}$ along the unrefracted pointing vector—into true refracted seafloor coordinates (using a nominal refractive index $n_w \approx 1.34116$, while full dispersive hydrographic models distinguish the phase index $n_p \approx 1.3411$ for Snell ray bending from the group index $n_g \approx 1.3651$ for pulse propagation speed).
  * *Engineering & DEM Relevance:* Provides the standard refraction correction equations now implemented in open-source spaceborne bathymetry frameworks (`SlideRule Earth`, `PhoREAL`, `icepyx`, and the official `ATL24` bathymetric product) for calibrating satellite-derived bathymetry (SDB) and coastal DEMs in remote or denied regions.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 7, Chapter 9, Chapter 11.

* **Parsons, B., & Sclater, J. G.** (1977). *An analysis of the variation of ocean floor bathymetry and heat flow with age.* *Journal of Geophysical Research*, 82(5), 803-827. DOI: [10.1029/JB082i005p00803](https://doi.org/10.1029/JB082i005p00803).
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** Derives the exact one-dimensional conductive cooling and Pratt isostatic subsidence solutions for oceanic lithosphere created at a mid-ocean ridge crest ($d_0 \approx 2{,}500\text{ m}$). Proves that for young lithosphere ($t_{\text{age}} < 70\text{ Myr}$), the thermal boundary layer follows a half-space error-function cooling law where positive-downward basement depth $d(t_{\text{age}})$ below mean sea level (corresponding to seafloor elevation $z(t_{\text{age}}) = -d(t_{\text{age}})$) increases strictly with the square root of crustal age, whereas for older seafloor ($t_{\text{age}} > 20\text{–}70\text{ Myr}$), basal mantle heat supply causes depth to approach an asymptotic thermal plate equilibrium exponentially:
    $$d(t_{\text{age}}) = -z(t_{\text{age}}) = \begin{cases} 2500 + 350\sqrt{t_{\text{age}}}, & 0 \le t_{\text{age}} \le 70\text{ Myr} \\[4pt] 6400 - 3200\exp\left(-\dfrac{t_{\text{age}}}{62.8}\right), & t_{\text{age}} > 20\text{ Myr} \end{cases} \quad (\text{depth } d \text{ in m, } t_{\text{age}} \text{ in Myr})$$
  * **Validation and Error Budget:** Fits sediment-stripped North Pacific and North Atlantic bathymetric profiles within $\pm 80\text{–}100\text{ m}$ RMS across $0\text{–}160\text{ Myr}$, constraining an asymptotic thermal plate thickness of $a \approx 125 \pm 10\text{ km}$ and basal mantle temperature $T_m \approx 1350^\circ\text{C}$ (later recalibrated for hotter, thinner $95\text{ km}$ plates in the `GDH1` model of Stein & Stein, 1992).
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** The fundamental geophysical law governing first-order global ocean depth (explaining nearly $4{,}000\text{ m}$ of abyssal relief), used directly in `GMT grdpmodeler` to subtract normal thermal subsidence from global bathymetric DEMs (`GEBCO`, `SRTM15+`) to isolate residual dynamic topography, mantle plumes, and hotspot swells.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 4.

* **Paull, L., Saeedi, S., Seto, M., & Li, H.** (2014). *AUV Navigation and Localization: A Review.* *IEEE Journal of Oceanic Engineering*, 39(1), 131-149. DOI: [10.1109/JOE.2013.2278891](https://doi.org/10.1109/JOE.2013.2278891).
  * **Platform & Kinematic Focus:** Reviews acoustic and inertial underwater positioning architectures—Ultra-Short Baseline (USBL), Long Baseline (LBL), Doppler Velocity Log (DVL) bottom-lock aiding, and Bathymetric SLAM—for submerged AUVs, ROVs, and deep-tow sleds deprived of GNSS signals.
  * **DEM Uncertainty & Error Budget Implications:** Quantifies unbounded horizontal uncertainty growth ($\text{THU}$ drifting at $0.05\%\text{–}0.2\%$ of distance traveled under DVL-INS dead reckoning) and demonstrates how cross-line terrain contour matching (`mbnavadjust` / Bathy-SLAM) eliminates duplicate ghosting of steep abyssal fault scarps.
  * **Validation & Operational Guidance:** Guides the selection of acoustic aiding (shipboard USBL vs. seabed LBL transponder arrays) and reciprocal cross-line spacing to bound subsea trajectory covariance.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 5, Chapter 7.

* **Pavlis, N. K., Holmes, S. A., Kenyon, S. C., & Factor, J. K.** (2012). *The development and evaluation of the Earth Gravitational Model 2008 (EGM2008).* *Journal of Geophysical Research: Solid Earth*, 117(B4), 2011JB008916. DOI: [10.1029/2011JB008916](https://doi.org/10.1029/2011JB008916).
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** Documents the mathematical formulation, data fusion, and global validation of the $2159 \times 2159$ spherical harmonic geopotential model (with additional coefficients extending to degree 2190), achieving a $5' \times 5'$ ($\sim 9\text{ km}$ at the equator) spatial resolution. EGM2008 combined long-wavelength satellite gravimetry from the GRACE mission (`ITG-GRACE03S`) with a global $5'$ terrestrial, airborne, and altimetry-derived gravity anomaly database using ellipsoidal harmonic analysis and least-squares synthesis.
  * **Validation and Error Budget:** Evaluates both commission errors (propagated harmonic coefficient covariance) and omission errors (unmodeled short-wavelength topographic gravity above degree 2160). Against 12,336 independent GPS/leveling benchmarks worldwide, EGM2008 achieved an overall root-mean-square (RMS) geoid undulation accuracy of $\pm 10.3\text{ cm}$ (improving to $\pm 5\text{–}8\text{ cm}$ in well-surveyed lowlands of North America, Europe, and Australia, while degrading to $>25\text{ cm}$ in rugged, un-surveyed mountain belts).
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** Serves as the standard global geoid reference surface (`EPSG:3855`) for converting ellipsoidal heights ($h$) to orthometric heights ($H = h - N$) in global spaceborne DEM products, including Copernicus DEM, TanDEM-X, and ICESat-2 (`ATL08`).
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 3.

* **Pe'eri, S., & Philpot, W.** (2007). *Increasing the Existence of Very Shallow-Water LIDAR Measurements Using the Red-Channel Waveforms.* *IEEE Transactions on Geoscience and Remote Sensing*, 45(5), 1217-1223. DOI: [10.1109/TGRS.2007.894584](https://doi.org/10.1109/TGRS.2007.894584).
  * Referenced in Section 09.02 (Chapter 9) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 9.

* **Peano, G.** (1890). *Sur une courbe, qui remplit toute une aire plane.* *Mathematische Annalen*, 36(1), 157-160. DOI: [10.1007/BF01199438](https://doi.org/10.1007/BF01199438).
  * *The historic discovery of the first base-3 continuous space-filling curve, proving that 1D continuous curves can map surjectively onto 2D areas.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 16.

* **Pearson, C., & Snay, R.** (2013). *Introducing HTDP 3.1 to transform coordinates across time and spatial reference frames.* *GPS Solutions*, 17(1), 1-15. DOI: [10.1007/s10291-012-0255-y](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10291-012-0255-y).
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** Documents the architecture of NOAA's **Horizontal Time-Dependent Positioning (HTDP)** software (originally introduced as `HTDP 1.0` by Richard Snay in 1992 and evolved through `HTDP 3.1` to modern `HTDP 3.4+` / `IFDM2022`), which bridges global ITRF/IGS realizations and North American/Pacific NAD83 realizations (`NAD83(2011)`, `NAD83(PA11)`, `NAD83(MA11)`). Combines rigid Euler-pole rotations outside deformation zones with bilinearly interpolated 3D secular velocity grids inside the Western United States and Alaska, analytical Okada (1985) elastic dislocation patches for over 30 historical earthquakes (from 1906 San Francisco $M_w\ 7.9$ and 1964 Prince William Sound $M_w\ 9.2$ to 2010 El Mayor–Cucapah $M_w\ 7.2$), and empirical postseismic decay models.
  * **Validation and Error Budget:** Demonstrates horizontal velocity interpolation accuracies ($1\sigma$) of $\pm 1\text{–}2\text{ mm yr}^{-1}$ across California, Oregon, Washington, and Alaska, reducing decade-scale unmodeled coordinate drift (THU) from $30\text{–}50\text{ cm}$ down to $<2\text{ cm}$.
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** The operational engine (embedded directly in `NOAA VDatum`, `OPUS`, `Esri ArcGIS Pro`, `Trimble Business Center`, and `PROJ`) required to propagate historical USGS 3DEP LiDAR point clouds and NOAA hydrographic surveys across the San Andreas and Cascadia plate boundaries to a unified target epoch prior to DEM mosaicking or differencing.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 4.

* **Pebesma, E. J.** (2004). *Multivariable geostatistics in S: the gstat package.* *Computers & Geosciences*, 30(7), 683-691. DOI: [10.1016/j.cageo.2004.03.012](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cageo.2004.03.012).
  * Primary citation for `gstat`, detailing open-source variogram modeling, Ordinary Kriging, and conditional simulation routines in R.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 33.

* **Peltier, W. R., Argus, D. F., & Drummond, R.** (2015). *Space geodesy constrains ice age terminal deglaciation: The global ICE‐6G_C (VM5a) model.* *Journal of Geophysical Research: Solid Earth*, 120(1), 450-487. DOI: [10.1002/2014JB011176](https://doi.org/10.1002/2014JB011176).
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** Formulates the global **Glacial Isostatic Adjustment (GIA)** model `ICE-6G_C` paired with the radial multi-layer Maxwell viscoelastic mantle viscosity profile `VM5a`. Solves the gravitationally self-consistent sea-level equation and viscoelastic Love-number load response to the disintegration of the Laurentide, Fennoscandian, Barents–Kara, and Antarctic ice sheets since the Last Glacial Maximum ($\sim 26\text{–}21\text{ ka}$), tightly constrained by a global network of three-dimensional GNSS station velocities and Holocene relative sea-level curves.
  * **Validation and Error Budget:** Accurately predicts present-day vertical crustal uplift domes ($\dot{h}_{\text{GIA}}$) peaking at $+13\text{ to }+15\text{ mm yr}^{-1}$ around Hudson Bay and $+10\text{ to }+11\text{ mm yr}^{-1}$ in the Gulf of Bothnia, peripheral forebulge collapse of $-1.5\text{ to }-2.5\text{ mm yr}^{-1}$ along the U.S. Mid-Atlantic coast, radial outward horizontal velocities of $1\text{–}3\text{ mm yr}^{-1}$, and the secular geoid undulation rate $\dot{N}_{\text{GIA}} \approx 0.06\,\dot{h}_{\text{GIA}}$ ($\sim +1.0\text{ to }+1.5\text{ mm yr}^{-1}$), yielding net orthometric height rates $\dot{H}_{\text{GIA}} = \dot{h}_{\text{GIA}} - \dot{N}_{\text{GIA}}$.
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** Supplies the continental-scale vertical crustal velocity ($\dot{h}_{\text{GIA}}$) and dynamic geoid rate ($\dot{N}_{\text{GIA}}$) grids essential for propagating legacy leveling datums (`NGVD29`, `NAVD88`, `CGVD28`) into modern time-dependent geopotential datums (`NAPGD2022` / `SGEOID2022` / `DGEOID2022`, `CGVD2013`, `EVRF2019`) and for separating land subsidence from eustatic sea-level rise in coastal inundation DEMs.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 4.

* **Pérez, P., Gangnet, M., & Blake, A.** (2003). *Poisson image editing.* *ACM Transactions on Graphics*, 22(3), 313-318. DOI: [10.1145/882262.882269](https://doi.org/10.1145/882262.882269).
  * *Derives variational gradient-domain Poisson editing ($\nabla^2 z = \nabla\cdot\mathbf{g}$ with Dirichlet boundary conditions) and mixed-gradient guidance fields for seamless local-to-regional surface insertion.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 23.

* **Peters, R., Ledoux, H., & Meijers, M.** (2014). *A Voronoi-Based Approach to Generating Depth-Contours for Hydrographic Charts.* *Marine Geodesy*, 37(2), 145-166. DOI: [10.1080/01490419.2014.902882](https://doi.org/10.1080/01490419.2014.902882).
  * Rigorous mathematical analysis of the asymmetric, shoal-safe bathymetric contour generalization problem, formulating algorithmic guarantees that generalized isobaths displace strictly seaward into deeper water.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 26.

* **Petit, G., & Luzum, B. (Eds.).** (2010). *IERS Conventions (2010)* (IERS Technical Note No. 36). Frankfurt am Main: Verlag des Bundesamts für Kartographie und Geodäsie.
  * **Scope:** ISBN: `978-3-89888-989-6` | International Earth Rotation and Reference Systems Service (IERS) Standards.
  * **Critical Annotation:** Defines the authoritative international standards for terrestrial and celestial reference systems (ITRS/GCRS), Earth orientation parameters (polar motion, UT1, precession-nutation), solid Earth tides (permanent tide conventions: conventional tide-free, mean-tide, and zero-tide), and ocean tidal loading.
  * **Key Formulations & Error Budgets:** Quantifies how the permanent zonal lunisolar tidal potential $\Delta W_{\text{tide}}(\phi)$ induces a solid-Earth crustal radial displacement $\Delta h_{\text{crust}} = h_{\text{mean}} - h_{\text{tide-free}} = h_2 \Delta W_{\text{tide}}/\gamma_0$ (spanning $\sim -12\text{ cm}$ at the poles to $+6\text{ cm}$ at the equator for Love number $h_2 \approx 0.608$), a geoid undulation shift $\Delta N_{\text{mean} - \text{tide-free}} = (1 + k_2)\Delta W_{\text{tide}}/\gamma_0$ (with $k_2 \approx 0.302$), and a net orthometric/leveled height shift $\Delta H = \Delta h_{\text{crust}} - \Delta N = -(1 + k_2 - h_2)\Delta W_{\text{tide}}/\gamma_0$.
  * **Practitioner Insight:** Crucial for resolving centimeter- to decimeter-level permanent tide vertical discrepancies when combining conventional tide-free ITRF/GNSS trajectories with zero-tide gravimetric geoid models or mean-tide oceanographic satellite altimetry surfaces.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 2.

* **Peucker, T. K., & Chrisman, N.** (1975). *Cartographic Data Structures.* *The American Cartographer*, 2(1), 55-69. DOI: [10.1559/152304075784447289](https://doi.org/10.1559/152304075784447289).
  * *Early foundational work detailing the transition from unorganized spaghetti vector drafting lines to structured topological networks and TINs.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 17.

* **Peucker, T. K., Fowler, R. J., Little, J. J., & Mark, D. M.** (1978). The triangulated irregular network. *Proceedings of the Digital Terrain Models (DTM) Symposium*, American Society of Photogrammetry, St. Louis, MO, 516–532.
  * *The foundational automated-cartography paper defining the Triangulated Irregular Network (`TIN`), Very Important Points (`VIPs`), and topological hard breaklines.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 15.

* **Philpot, W. (Ed.).** (2019). *Airborne Laser Hydrography II*. Ithaca, NY: Cornell University Library eCommons. https://hdl.handle.net/1813/66666
  * *Core Physical & Mathematical Contribution:* Bridges classical underwater radiative transfer theory with modern dual-wavelength ($1064\text{ nm} / 532\text{ nm}$) topo-bathymetric LiDAR hardware. Derives the analytical form of the bathymetric lidar waveform—separating the air–water interface return, the exponentially decaying volume backscatter curve governed by the system attenuation coefficient $k_{\text{sys}} \in [K_d, c]$, and the broadened benthic bottom pulse.
  * *Engineering & DEM Relevance:* Explicitly addresses the "white ribbon" shallow-water blind zone ($d \lesssim 0.25\text{–}0.40\text{ m}$) where surface and bottom echoes overlap within the laser pulse width, receiver Field-of-View (FOV) selection to balance multiple-scattering energy collection against solar background noise, and benthic pseudo-reflectance retrieval.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 9.

* **Pierzchała, M., Giguère, P., & Astrup, R.** (2018). *Mapping forests using an unmanned ground vehicle with 3D LiDAR and graph-SLAM.* *Computers and Electronics in Agriculture*, 145, 217-225. DOI: [10.1016/j.compag.2017.12.034](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.compag.2017.12.034).
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** Foundational empirical and methodological evaluations of **Personal and Mobile Laser Scanning (`PLS` / `MLS`) with 3D Graph-SLAM** beneath dense forest canopies where multipath and crown attenuation degrade GNSS RTK fixes to meter-level errors. Pierzchała et al. (2018) demonstrate how extracting vertical tree-stem cylinders and ground-surface facets as persistent landmarks in a 3D pose graph constrains sub-canopy UGV trajectories, while Gollob et al. (2020) rigorously benchmark handheld SLAM (`GeoSLAM ZEB Horizon`) across multi-species forest inventory plots using multi-loop figure-8 walking patterns.
  * **Validation and Error Budget:** Prove that closed-loop figure-8 trajectories under dense canopy achieve $\pm 1\text{–}3\text{ cm}$ relative horizontal/vertical accuracy, $>95\%$ stem detection completeness, and $\pm 1.5\text{–}2.3\text{ cm}$ RMSE in Diameter at Breast Height (`DBH`) and bare-earth micro-topography—satisfying **USGS 3DEP Lidar Base Specification (`LBS`)** Quality Level 1/2 Non-Vegetated and Vegetated Vertical Accuracy ($\text{NVA}_{95\%} = 1.96\,\text{RMSE}_z \le 19.6\text{ cm}$, $\text{VVA}_{95\%} \le 29.4\text{ cm}$) and **ASPRS Positional Accuracy Standards** when anchored to surveyed ground control and filtered for dynamic wind-swaying foliage.
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** Establishes the standard operational field protocol (closed perimeter loops with interior cross-ties and at least $3\text{–}4$ non-collinear total-station/RTK ground control targets) for sub-canopy forest DEM generation and ecological structural monitoring.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 6.

* **Pike, R. J., Evans, I. S., & Hengl, T.** (2009). *Chapter 1 Geomorphometry: A Brief Guide.* *Developments in Soil Science*, 3-30. DOI: [10.1016/S0166-2481(08)00001-9](https://doi.org/10.1016/S0166-2481(08)00001-9).
  * **Scope:** ISBN: `978-0-12-374345-9` | ISSN: `0166-2481` | (Ch. 1) | Algorithmic Geomorphometry & Open-Source/Commercial Toolboxes.
  * **Critical Annotation:** Compiled by leading developers of the international geomorphometry community, this comprehensive 772-page handbook synthesizes the mathematical theory of land-surface parameters with practical software implementations in GRASS GIS, SAGA GIS, RiverTools, and ArcGIS. It provides deep comparative analyses of gridding and geostatistical interpolation estimators, pit-removal and drainage enforcement algorithms, and automated landform classification.
  * **Practitioner Insight:** Guides the design of multi-step DEM processing pipelines through explicit treatment of error propagation via Monte Carlo simulation and empirical benchmarks of algorithm performance across varying terrain roughness regimes.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 1.

* **Pingel, T. J., Clarke, K. C., & McBride, W. A.** (2013). *An improved simple morphological filter for the terrain classification of airborne LIDAR data.* *ISPRS Journal of Photogrammetry and Remote Sensing*, 77, 21-30. DOI: [10.1016/j.isprsjprs.2012.12.002](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.isprsjprs.2012.12.002).
  * *Core Mathematical Contribution:* Develops the Simple Morphological Filter (`SMRF`), which applies linearly increasing disk-shaped structuring elements ($r_k = k \cdot \Delta x$) with a constant slope threshold $s_{\max}$ (`d_max = s_max * r_k`) to a minimum-surface raster, followed by thin-plate spline interpolation and point-to-surface residual thresholding.
  * *Engineering & DEM Relevance:* Implemented natively in `PDAL` (`filters.smrf`), `SMRF` achieves high classification accuracy across complex urban and forested terrain with only four intuitive physical parameters (`cell`, `slope`, `window`, `threshold`).
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 9, Chapter 21.

* **Pitman, W. C., & Heirtzler, J. R.** (1966). *Magnetic Anomalies over the Pacific-Antarctic Ridge.* *Science*, 154(3753), 1164-1171. DOI: [10.1126/science.154.3753.1164](https://doi.org/10.1126/science.154.3753.1164).
  * Referenced in Section 04.03 (Chapter 4) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 4.

* **Poland, J. F., Lofgren, B. E., Ireland, R. L., & Pugh, R. G.** (1975). *Land subsidence in the San Joaquin Valley, California, as of 1972.* *Professional Paper*. DOI: [10.3133/pp437H](https://doi.org/10.3133/pp437H).
  * Referenced in Section 04.02 (Chapter 4) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 4.

* **Pope, R. M., & Fry, E. S.** (1997). *Absorption spectrum (380–700 nm) of pure water II Integrating cavity measurements.* *Applied Optics*, 36(33), 8710. DOI: [10.1364/AO.36.008710](https://doi.org/10.1364/AO.36.008710).
  * Referenced in Section 09.02 (Chapter 9) for foundational mathematical, physical, or observational methodology.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 9.

* **Puente, I., González-Jorge, H., Martínez-Sánchez, J., & Arias, P.** (2013). *Review of mobile mapping and surveying technologies.* *Measurement*, 46(7), 2127-2145. DOI: [10.1016/j.measurement.2013.03.006](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.measurement.2013.03.006).
  * **Platform & Kinematic Focus:** Dissects the hardware architecture of automotive and rail-based Mobile Mapping Systems (MMS), comparing tactical-grade and navigation-grade IMUs, dual-frequency GNSS receivers, wheel-mounted Distance Measurement Indicators (DMIs), and multi-head LiDAR profilers.
  * **DEM Uncertainty & Error Budget Implications:** Derives first-order uncertainty propagation from boresight angular misalignments ($\delta\phi, \delta\theta, \delta\psi$) and lever-arm offsets across highway corridor speeds, explaining why uncompensated IMU gyro drift during urban-canyon GNSS outages manifests as low-frequency vertical warping ($\text{TVU}$ drift) and multi-pass curb/pavement step offsets.
  * **Validation & Operational Guidance:** Benchmarks commercial MMS configurations (Optech Lynx, Riegl VMX, Trimble MX) against static terrestrial control networks.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 7.

* **Pugh, D., & Woodworth, P.** (2014). *Sea-Level Science: Understanding Tides, Surges, Tsunamis and Mean Sea-Level Changes.* *Cambridge University Press*. DOI: [10.1017/CBO9781139235778](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9781139235778).
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** A rigorous physical oceanography textbook unifying equilibrium and dynamic tide theory, amphidromic Kelvin wave propagation on continental shelves, meteorological storm surges (inverted barometer effect $\Delta\eta_{\text{IB}} \approx -0.995\text{ cm hPa}^{-1}\Delta P_a$ and wind-stress setup $\tau_w / (\rho_w g D)$), Mean Dynamic Topography ($\text{MDT} = \text{MSS} - N$), and secular relative sea-level rise.
  * **Key Formulation and Chapter Pointers:** Chapters 1, 7, and 10 derive the surface geostrophic balance relating sea-surface slope to permanent ocean boundary currents:
    $$f\mathbf{k} \times \mathbf{u}_g = -g\nabla_H\text{MDT} = -g\nabla_H(\text{MSS} - N)$$
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** Bridges the conceptual divide between geodesists and oceanographers by explaining why steric density gradients and western boundary currents (e.g., the Gulf Stream and Kuroshio) sustain $1.0\text{–}1.5\text{ m}$ of permanent sea-surface topography, proving why LMSL is never an equipotential surface.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 3.

* **Raney, R. K.** (1998). *The delay/Doppler radar altimeter.* *IEEE Transactions on Geoscience and Remote Sensing*, 36(5), 1578-1588. DOI: [10.1109/36.718861](https://doi.org/10.1109/36.718861).
  * *Core Invention Contribution:* Conceptualizes the **Delay-Doppler (SAR) Altimeter**, proving that coherent pulse burst transmission and along-track Doppler beam sharpening collapses the along-track footprint by a factor of 10 while boosting signal-to-noise ratio.
  * *Engineering & DEM Relevance:* Led directly to the design of ESA CryoSat-2 (SIRAL), Sentinel-3 (SRAL), and Sentinel-6 Michael Freilich, enabling precision altimetry over coastal waters, sea ice leads, and narrow continental rivers.

DOI: [10.1109/ICRA.2016.7487628](https://doi.org/10.1109/ICRA.2016.7487628).

* **Renard, V., & Allenou, J. P.** (1979). Sea Beam, multi-beam echo-sounding in "Jean Charcot": Description, evaluation and first results. *International Hydrographic Review*, 56(1), 35–67.
  * *Core Engineering Contribution:* Details the technical installation, acoustic performance, and operational results of the first commercial deep-sea multibeam system installed aboard a civilian oceanographic vessel.
  * *Engineering & DEM Relevance:* Demonstrates the transformative power of continuous swath bathymetry in resolving deep-sea fracture zones, seamount volcanism, and plate tectonic boundaries.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 10.

* **Reuter, H. I., Nelson, A., & Jarvis, A.** (2007). *An evaluation of void‐filling interpolation methods for SRTM data.* *International Journal of Geographical Information Science*, 21(9), 983-1008. DOI: [10.1080/13658810601169899](https://doi.org/10.1080/13658810601169899).
  * *Systematic geomorphic and Void-Size-Stratified benchmark of Kriging, IDW, spline, ANUDEM, and auxiliary delta-fill interpolation across SRTM mountain and desert voids.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 21.

* **Rew, R., & Davis, G.** (1990). *NetCDF: an interface for scientific data access.* *IEEE Computer Graphics and Applications*, 10(4), 76-82. DOI: [10.1109/38.56302](https://doi.org/10.1109/38.56302).
  * *Introduces the machine-independent Network Common Data Form (NetCDF) array architecture widely used for oceanographic and geophysical elevation grids.*
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 19.

* **Rice, G., & Riley, J.** (2011). Measuring the water level datum relative to the ellipsoid during hydrographic survey. *Proceedings of the U.S. Hydrographic Conference 2011*, Tampa, FL.
  * **Scope and Theoretical Contribution:** Operationalizes ERS for national hydrographic charting (codified in the NOAA *Hydrographic Surveys Specifications and Deliverables [HSSD]* and *Field Procedures Manual [FPM]*) by quantifying how GNSS-buoy campaigns, vessel-based dynamic draft calibrations, and hydrodynamic SEP surfaces (`VDatum`) satisfy International Hydrographic Organization (IHO) S-44 Special Order and Order 1a Total Vertical Uncertainty ($\text{TVU}_{95\%} = \sqrt{a^2 + (b \cdot d)^2}$) tolerances for IHO S-100 / S-102 bathymetric surface deliverables.
  * **Relevance to DEM Practitioners:** Details practical quality-assurance diagnostics (such as crossline comparison on the ellipsoid vs. chart datum) to isolate GNSS trajectory dropouts from hydrodynamic separation model biases in coastal bathymetric grids.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 3.

* **Rignot, E., Echelmeyer, K., & Krabill, W.** (2001). *Penetration depth of interferometric synthetic‐aperture radar signals in snow and ice.* *Geophysical Research Letters*, 28(18), 3501-3504. DOI: [10.1029/2000GL012484](https://doi.org/10.1029/2000GL012484).
  * Quantifies multi-meter microwave radar penetration depths into dry polar firn and glacier ice by comparing C-/L-band InSAR phase centers against airborne laser altimetry.
  * **Handbook Cross-References:** Cited in Chapter 30.

* **Rinckes, D., & Bunge, P.** (2014). *Open Location Code: An Open Source Standard for Addresses, Independent of Building Numbers and Street Names*. Google Zürich Engineering Technical Specification. GitHub repository: `https://github.com/google/open-location-code`.
  * *Defines the base-20 alphanumeric character set, hierarchical prefix scaling, and offline encoding algorithm for Plus Codes.*

J., et al.** (2017). *Cross‐validation strategies for data with temporal, spatial, hierarchical, or phylogenetic structure.* *Ecography*, 40(8), 913-929. DOI: [10.1111/ecog.02881](https://doi.org/10.1111/ecog.02881).
  * *Establishes the statistical necessity of spatial block cross-validation with variogram-derived exclusion buffers to prevent spatial autocorrelation data leakage in geospatial machine learning models.*

* **Robin, C., Nudds, S., MacAulay, P., Godin, A., De Lange Boom, B., & Bartlett, J.** (2016). *Hydrographic Vertical Separation Surfaces (HyVSEPs) for the Tidal Waters of Canada.* *Marine Geodesy*, 39(2), 195-222. DOI: [10.1080/01490419.2016.1160011](https://doi.org/10.1080/01490419.2016.1160011).

* **Rolstad, C., Haug, T., & Denby, B.** (2009). *Spatially integrated geodetic glacier mass balance and its uncertainty based on geostatistical analysis: application to the western Svartisen ice cap, Norway.* *Journal of Glaciology*, 55(192), 666-680. DOI: [10.3189/002214309789470950](https://doi.org/10.3189/002214309789470950).
